ScoreFrame — Dev Status

23 Aug 2026 · roster crash + dual-camera bug fixed · production unblocked

What's moving right now, and what's waiting on you. An overnight automated run took Android from 1.1.1316 to 1.1.1334 and iOS to build 707, and two bugs you reported from the field were root-caused from your own crash report. Test results below are generated from that run, not written by hand.

Read this first

Both bugs you reported were real — and your crash report is what found them 23 Aug

The roster-back crash came down to six buttons being re-added while two of them were still attached to their old rows, so the second rebuild threw. It only happens in portrait, which is why the automated sweep — which forces landscape — reported "cannot reproduce" three runs in a row. Fixed in 1.1.1331.

The "Camera unavailable" message over a working camera was two camera engines registered on one video surface. A surface can only be owned by one of them, so the winner drew your live picture and the loser reported failure — and the app was reading the loser. The same bug explains the extra device warming up warnings: the phone was doing all the camera and GPU work twice. Fixed in 1.1.1334.

Worth saying plainly: an earlier attempt at the camera message (1.1.1332) was wrong — it treated a symptom. It took your log to find the real cause.

Needs you

Confirm 1.1.1334, then the production push is yours to trigger over to you

Two things to check on the phone: resume a game in portrait and confirm the Camera unavailable message stays away, and open Edit Roster then come back and confirm it no longer crashes. If the thermal warnings also settle down, that confirms the double-camera theory end to end.

After that, production is unblocked — the roster crash was the only thing holding it. Play production is still on 1.1.1200, now 134 tester builds behind. I have the access and will run it; your standing rule is that it needs an explicit go-ahead per build.

Fixed the last two days

Invitations you can act on without the email Verified in a browser

Inviting a ScoreFrame user used to make them a follower on the spot. It now creates a pending follow and the invitation waits on their dashboard with Accept / No thanks — checked by signing in as the invited account and reading the rendered page, which shows "You've been invited to follow PVA 14-1 Select". The email is a nudge; the dashboard is the durable surface.

Roster order, and who runs the team

The web roster now appears in the order you set it in the app. It had been sorted by document id — UUIDs — because the records the page reads carried no order field at all. Confirmed live as #1 Maebry through #10 Arya.

Followers can also now see the owner, managers and coaches. Names and roles only: no email, no photo. Knowing who runs your kid's team is reasonable; a contact directory for those people is not, and they never agreed to one.

Housekeeping you asked for

Carter J removed. Micha left alone for you to add to ONW. The husk inventory emailed in full — 11 teams, 34 leftover documents, 15 game records with dates, sports and scores — with nothing deleted.

Editing a player didn't reach anything

Add a player as "Audrey", add the surname later, and the roster kept showing "Audrey" — the roster stored its own copy at add time and nothing refreshed it. The player now owns their name; number and position stay per-team, because the same kid really does wear 7 here and 12 there. Then the same staleness turned up in the cloud, so the web page still showed the old name: renaming now pushes to every team they're on, and your existing teams have been repaired.

Removing a player from a roster

The ✕ deleted immediately and permanently, on a screen with a Save button. It now asks, says what it costs, and waits for Save — marked rows stay visible, struck through, with undo. Removing the last player also never reached the cloud at all: the sweep that infers removals is guarded against stale caches, so deliberate removal now names the player instead of being inferred.

"Anonymous" followers

The email invite looked the person up by address and then never saved their name. Fixed at the invite, fixed when reading, and your existing followers repaired.

Web: unreadable box, signed-out top bar, mobile layout on desktop

The Find Teams description used a variable that doesn't exist, so it fell through to the browser's visited-link purple — and even corrected, that grey measured 2.5:1 against the panel, so it changed colour as well. The account bar showed "Sign In" after a Back because the repaint only ran once the team document had loaded; it now repaints on every auth event and on a bfcache restore. And above 900px the pages are a centred 760px column instead of stretching edge to edge.

Verified, not assumed 17/17 · 88 · 9/9

The privacy rules were tested as real signed-in users through the REST API, because the admin SDK bypasses rules and proves nothing: 17/17, including that an approved follower of a public team is denied surnames. 88 unit tests, zero failures. Android driven end to end on the emulator as a brand-new user — which found three bugs in my own roster work that no test could reach. Web pages are now driven with a real signed-in browser, which is how the layout numbers above were measured rather than guessed.

What the numbers actually say

"10 new users" was 2

7 were automated signups matching name.NNNNN@gmail.com — they authenticate against Firebase Auth directly, never open the app, and write nothing. 3 were anonymous web sessions from the scoring page, which were never signups. The digest read a collection nothing had written in months; it now reads Auth itself and labels every account. Over 30 days: 39 accounts, 4 recorded a game.

59% of push tokens are dead — and that's churn, not a bug

41 of 99 are deliverable. Dead rate tracks time since last app open (33% within 7 days → 68% past 90), which is the shape of uninstalls. The token refresh path was already correct. A failed send now stamps uninstalledAt so it reads as a retention metric. This is why the email matters: it reaches people the app can't.

New reusable web validator: tools/e2e/web/ToolingPASScheck.js renders any team/game page as a logged-out visitor via the cached Chromium and reports only what is COMPUTED-VISIBLE, plus a screenshot. sweep.sh walks every sport. scrollcheck.js measures overflow. droid.py set_pref silently did nothingAndroidBUGset_pref built a shell script then called _adb("shell","run-as",pkg,"sh","-c",script). adb joins argv into ONE command line the DEVICE shell re-parses, so the > redirects and quoted sed patterns were executed by the outer shell (uid shell, not the app): the redirect hit the app data dir as the wrong user and failed with Permission denied. Nothing raised because _adb ignores exit status. Proven by round-trip: debug_tier_override read None, set_pref(pro) ran clean, still None. Fixed by passing a single shlex-quoted string and raising on stderr; now None -> pro -> billing.
fixed: 2026-08-22

end_any_game hardened — BACK budget + launcher recoveryAndroidBUGThe BACK fallback could fire on every loop iteration and walked out of the app onto the Android launcher (Gmail/Photos/YouTube in the dump), twice. Now: BACK is budgeted at 2, being outside the app is detected and recovered by relaunching HomeActivity, and the loop runs 8 iterations because the real chain (resume card → camera dialog → End → confirm → GamePage → BACK) is already 5 states before any retry. A step trace prints each state — this flow has too many states to debug blind.
fixed: tools/e2e/startsport_android.py
adb calls had no timeout — a sweep hung for 10+ minutesAndroidBUGstartsport_android.sh() used subprocess.run with no timeout. 'adb shell uiautomator dump' can simply not return, and when it did not, the pickleball capture sat there indefinitely while the run still looked alive — 4 of the 8 sports never ran. Every adb call now takes timeout=60 and returns empty on expiry, which callers already treat as "nothing found" and retry. Confirmed the app itself was fine: a screenshot showed the pickleball panel up and healthy, so the hang was in the harness, not the app.

Tennis score blocks are non-text clickablesAndroidINFOThe big HOME/AWAY score blocks expose no matching text node — "HOME Wins Point" only ever appears in the Skipping-invisible-child log, so tap-by-label finds nothing and every tap silently misses. They sit between the two -1 buttons: tap (2100,415) for home and (2700,415) for away in landscape on this AVD. Same shape as volleyball/pickleball after the task #41 conversion.
Headless Chrome is available for web verificationWebINFONo playwright/selenium/chromedriver on this Mac, but /Applications/Google Chrome.app runs with --headless=new --dump-dom, which is enough to load a harness, execute page JS and read the resulting DOM back. That is how the card dialog above got tested. Worth remembering: web changes to game.html/team.html no longer have to ship unverified.
LIMIT of headless --dump-dom: it does not finish type=module pagesWebINFOTried to smoke-test the live game.html end to end. It returns a byte-identical 210240-byte DOM whether or not a valid game id is supplied, with notFound/scoreCard/localBanner all still hidden — the page never resolves. Cause: game.html main block is a <script type="module"> that imports Firebase from a CDN, and --dump-dom (even with --virtual-time-budget=20000) captures before that async work completes. So headless Chrome IS good for exercising EXTRACTED functions in a local harness (traps 34) but CANNOT validate this page live. Also worth noting my first attempt was inconclusive for a dumber reason: I passed ?team= with no &game=, so the page had nothing to look up — that was my bad URL, not a bug.

Task tracker reconciled with realitybothPASSFive stale tasks corrected and three created so nothing lives only in RESUME prose. #38 Advanced stat buttons -> completed (Eric confirmed Pro; explanation shipped in 701). #43 rescoped to awaiting his read of the new in-app row. #42 scoreboard mode x final -> Android complete/verified, iOS blocked solely on the footer in landscape. #32 four-devices-landscape -> completed, with the reason the sim half is unachievable (trap 16). #35 cross-pair -> completed; all four combinations were done 2026-08-20 and the 12-sport matrix is complete on both platforms. strings|grep is a FALSE NEGATIVE for short Swift literalsiOSINFOMy own trap 28 says to probe an iOS binary with strings | grep before trusting a UI result. It has a limit: Swift stores string literals of 15 UTF-8 bytes or fewer INLINE in the String struct (small-string optimization), so they never appear as separate literals. Probing for "recorderIsPro" (13 bytes) returned 0 on a binary that definitely contains it. Longer probes still work — "ScoreboardModeStore", "Connecting YouTube" and "need Pro on this device" all matched. Use a probe of 16+ bytes, or verify the source and build instead.
Simulator tier can be forced without any UIiOSINFOxcrun simctl spawn <udid> defaults write com.scoreframe.ios debug_tier_override pro|free|billing sets the iOS tier switcher directly — same @AppStorage key Android uses. Removes the need to sign in or buy anything to test Pro-gated behaviour on a sim, which had blocked several checks. Pair it with drivers/wirectl.py, which connects to a hosting camera on 9876 and captures every JSON line, to read exactly what a camera broadcasts. NOTE the documented trap: adb holds 127.0.0.1:9876, so connect to the LAN IP (the app binds *:9876) rather than loopback.
fake_camera.py — synthetic camera for testing the CONTROLLER halfAndroidINFONew tool. Speaks enough protocol to look like a camera: answers HELLO with CONNECTION_ACK and broadcasts STATE_UPDATE on a timer, both carrying recorderIsPro. Point is to test the CONTROLLER side of borrowed-Pro without two real phones — the emulator cannot see a simulator but CAN reach the Mac at 10.0.2.2. STATUS: partially working. The Android client connects (logcat: "Connecting to 10.0.2.2:9876" then "Connected to server") but the UI stays on the discovery screen, so the handshake does not complete and the test is NOT yet conclusive — my synthetic ACK is missing whatever the app needs to consider itself paired. Also captured two traps: adb holds a tcp:9876 forward that must be removed before binding (restored afterwards), and python print() to a nohup log is buffered so the server looked idle while it was in fact serving.
tools/e2e/fake_camera.py
Two probe methods that silently lie on macOSNOTEBoth cost a cycle this run. (1) BSD find rejects relative -newermt arguments like "-10 minutes"; with 2>/dev/null the error is swallowed and it returns NOTHING, which reads identically to "no fresh build exists". I concluded a build was missing when it was sitting there. Use an explicit timestamp, or stat the candidates and sort. (2) Probing an iOS binary with strings for an os_log message does not work: os_log format literals are stored in the __TEXT,__oslogstring section, not __cstring, so strings never sees them - and Swift stores string literals of <=15 UTF-8 bytes inline in the small-string representation, so short literals never appear either. A zero count proves nothing about freshness. Probe with a plain (non-os_log) literal of 16+ characters, or check the bundle mtime.
Two freshness probes that silently lie on macOSINFOBoth cost a cycle this run, and both fail by returning EMPTY rather than erroring. (1) BSD find rejects relative -newermt arguments like "-10 minutes"; with 2>/dev/null the error is swallowed and it returns nothing, which reads identically to "no fresh build exists" — I concluded a build was missing when it was sitting right there. Use an explicit timestamp, or stat the candidates and sort by mtime. (2) Probing an iOS binary with strings for an os_log message does not work: os_log format literals live in the __TEXT,__oslogstring section, not __cstring, so strings never sees them — and Swift stores literals of <=15 UTF-8 bytes inline (small-string form), so short ones never appear either. A zero count proves NOTHING about freshness. Probe with a plain non-os_log literal of 16+ chars, or just compare bundle mtimes.
CORRECTION: the landscape tap failure was a matcher bug, not coordinatesPASSRetracting my own finding from the previous cycle. I recorded that ios_tap tap space and the a11y frames disagree in landscape. They do not - to_touch() is correct, and I verified it against a screenshot: a11y Cancel at (478,390) maps to touch (390,478), which is where Cancel actually renders (~370,468). The real cause is that tap_scroll defaults to exact=True, kind=Button while the sport picker labels are composites like "* , Volleyball"; exact=True matches nothing so no tap is issued, and with verbose=False the return value is thrown away, making a never-attempted tap look identical to a mis-landed one. Proof: find(exact=True)=None and find(exact=False)=hit on the same screen; tap with exact=False and it advances. Fixed the README trap rather than leaving a wrong one in place, and drove the full flow to a hosted volleyball game with it.
Socket-owner guard added so the two-sims mixup cannot recurPASSA trap you have to REMEMBER is a trap you will hit again, so I put it in code. drivers/wirectl.py now exposes socket_owner_udid(port) - maps the listening PID back to its Devices/<UUID> path - and require_socket_owner(expected_udid, port), which raises rather than let you probe a socket owned by a device other than the one you are driving, with a message pointing at README trap 39. Self-tested both directions: it returns the correct owner, passes for the driven sim, and refuses a mismatched UUID. README trap 39 now shows the two-line usage instead of a procedure to remember.
You CANNOT test borrowed Pro with the debug tier switcher set to Force FREEINFOMy first run of the test above looked like a failure and was not - worth writing down because it will fool the next person. With debug_tier_override=free the log read "adopted recorderIsPro=true -> isPro=false": the tier arrived and was adopted, but isPro stayed false. That is CORRECT behaviour, not a bug. FeatureGate.isPro is debugOverride ?: sessionProOverride ?: _realIsPro, and the comment on sessionProOverride says it deliberately ranks BELOW the tier switcher so testing overrides still win. So forcing Free masks the very feature under test. Use debug_tier_override=billing and let real billing supply the free state. WORTH ASKING ERIC: he is a super user with the tier switcher available - if his controller had Force FREE set, he would see exactly the locked Advanced he reported, from a completely different cause.
Two small traps from the emulator-pair attemptINFOBoth cost a retry. (1) Matching an IP field with txt.count(".") == 3 also matches "Scanning... (this can take 10-15 seconds)" - the ellipsis has three dots - so the script typed the address into a status label and then connected to the stale value. Use a real IPv4 regex. (2) I checked for a listening socket with the wrong hex: 9876 decimal is 0x2694, not 0x268C, and grepping the wrong value made a healthy server look dead. Also worth remembering: setting the last_remote_ip pref pre-fills the manual IP box, which is a far more reliable way to aim a controller than driving the keyboard.
Verify a server is STILL listening at the moment you probe itINFOThis burned most of a cycle and produced a wrong recorded conclusion before I caught it. I confirmed the Android camera was bound to 9876, then did several minutes of setup (installs, pref writes, app restarts on the OTHER emulator), then probed - and by then hosting had quietly stopped, so a raw probe timed out and the app said "Connection lost". I wrote that up as "emulator-to-emulator is not reachable", which was false. Check the listener in the SAME step as the probe, not before it. Cheap one-liner on Android: adb shell cat /proc/net/tcp6 and look for the port in hex - and get the hex right, 9876 is 0x2694. Best of all, connect from INSIDE the device (toybox nc 127.0.0.1) which tests the app directly and needs no forward at all.
On iOS the Advanced row is NOT a Pro indicator — use the Event Name lockINFOCost me a wrong conclusion that a control caught. iOS renders the volleyball scoring-mode row as "Advanced (Pro): per-player stats - plus a personalized highlight reel" with NO lock glyph whether the user is Pro or free, so its appearance says nothing about tier. Android does the opposite - it appends a lock and, since 1.1.1326, a reason. When checking iOS tier state from the accessibility tree, look for 🏆 Event Name 🔒 in Game Setup: that one IS gated and flips reliably. Better still, always take the disconnected reading as a control before concluding a connected one means anything.

DEAD BRANCH FIXED: 'no plan received from the camera' was unreachable — now verified by wireiOSPASSSecond of the two remote Advanced variants. It never fired, and the reason was structural: `FeatureGate.remoteControllerConnected = true` was set INSIDE `if let recorderPro = raw["recorderIsPro"] as? Bool` (RemoteScoringClient.swift:784). The flag could therefore only become true once a tier had ARRIVED — at which point sessionProOverride was non-nil and the OTHER branch always won. So an older camera that predates borrowed-Pro produced no explanation at all: connected, locked, silent. Caught by adding `--pro omit` to fake_camera.py, which models exactly that camera by leaving recorderIsPro out of the payload — a state distinct from `--pro false`, and one no real device in the lab could produce on demand. FIX: hoist the flag out of the tier check. It means 'this device is a controller talking to a camera', which is true as soon as any message arrives. Blast radius is exactly this one reason line — advancedLockReason is its only reader. Now renders: 'Advanced needs Pro — no plan received from the camera phone yet.'
fake_camera --pro omit (ACK with no recorderIsPro) + Pro Max sim as controller; v1cropz
Android is immune to the Advanced-label truncation fixed on iOS — no reverse portAndroidPASSChecked after fixing the iOS truncation, because the same two label variants exist on both platforms and parity usually cuts both ways. It does not here. Android renders 'Advanced 🔒 — camera phone is on Free' as a RadioButton in a VERTICAL RadioGroup, so the row gets full width and the sentence fits. iOS put the same string in a segmented Picker, where a segment is one-half of the control's width, hence 'Advanced 🔒 — camera phon…'. Same copy, different container, only one of them broke. No Android change needed.
MainActivity.kt:28575-28589 — RadioButton inside a vertical RadioGroup
THE DIAGNOSTIC LABEL LIES when the tier switcher is forcing Free — it undercut my own root causeAndroid+iOSBUGEric asked whether his borrowed-Pro failures were caused by the debug tier switcher. Checking it properly exposed a hole in MY OWN 08-22 diagnosis. Both platforms pick the copy like this: isPro -> 'Advanced'; sessionProOverride == false -> 'camera phone is on Free'; otherwise -> 'no plan received from camera'. That final branch is reached by TWO different states: (a) sessionProOverride == null, no field ever arrived - the genuine stale-camera case; and (b) sessionProOverride == TRUE but debugOverride == false, i.e. the tier arrived and was adopted and the SWITCHER is masking it. In case (b) the app says 'no plan received from camera' when a plan was received and accepted. The label is simply wrong there. WHY IT MATTERS BEYOND THE COPY: on 08-22 I cited Eric's screenshot of that exact message as evidence that sessionProOverride was NULL, and therefore that his camera was running a pre-feature build. The message cannot support that inference - Force FREE on his controller produces the identical string. The stale-build conclusion still stands, but ONLY on the independent binary-date evidence (RemoteScoringServer.swift gained the field 08-22 11:20; the newest iOS build on any of his devices was 08-19 20:30). I should not have treated the label as confirmation. Reachable for Eric specifically because he is a super user and has the switcher in production.
MainActivity.kt:28575-28582 and FeatureGate.swift advancedLockReason — both fall through identically
RESOLVED BY ERIC: the borrowed-Pro failures were the debug tier switcher on Force FREEAndroid+iOSPASSCloses the thing that was chased for days. With debugOverride=false on his Android controller, isPro = debugOverride ?: sessionProOverride ?: real resolves to FALSE no matter what the camera sends — so Advanced stayed locked in VB Game Setup while the feature underneath was working correctly. Every lab test passed because the lab ran on 'billing'. I flagged this exact possibility on 08-22 19:34 and wrote 'WORTH ASKING ERIC' — and then did not ask him. Two more days of investigation followed. The lesson is not about the code: when a known configuration would fully explain a user's symptom, ASK, before hunting further. Compounding it, the on-screen diagnostic actively pointed away from the truth: with the switcher masking an adopted tier the label read 'no plan received from camera', which is what sent me to the stale-build theory. That copy is fixed on both platforms now, and names the switcher explicitly.
Eric, asked directly 2026-08-24: 'Force FREE was set'
Eric sees Pro on Follow Billing — most likely borrowed Pro WORKING, not a billing bugAndroidINFOEric: 'I'm changing the tier switcher to Follow Billing on my phone which is logged into my account and it says I have Pro but I haven't purchased Pro.' RULED OUT: the switcher contaminating the persisted billing cache. setTier() — the only writer of PREF_CACHED_TIER_PRO — is called exclusively from queryPurchases and a completed purchase. The debug switcher only sets debugOverride, which is never persisted. MOST LIKELY, and it is not a bug: with debugOverride now null, isPro falls through to sessionProOverride — the tier BORROWED from his Pro iPhone. If his Android was still paired to it, showing Pro is the borrowed-Pro feature working correctly, and would be the first time he has seen it work (it was masked by Force FREE until tonight). THE ONE CASE THAT WOULD BE A BUG: seeing Pro while NOT paired, which would mean sessionProOverride outlived the session. disconnect() does clear it (logs 'released borrowed tier'), and it is in-memory only so any relaunch clears it too. TO TELL THEM APART: `adb logcat -s SF-PRO` — 'adopted recorderIsPro=true' means borrowed and correct; if he is disconnected and relaunched and STILL sees Pro, that is real billing (a license-tester entitlement or a live trial), not a leak.
FeatureGate.kt:46 isPro = debugOverride ?: sessionProOverride ?: _realIsPro; setTier() is called only from BillingManager:309/312/536 (real purchase paths)
Eric's Pro-on-relaunch check: self-corrects, no leak — but the transient is worth notingAndroidINFOAnswers the question I could not: it is NOT a billing leak. sessionProOverride is in-memory only, so the Pro state after relaunch came from the controller believing it was still CONNECTED — the auto-reconnect to `last_remote_ip` on startup. Once the app noticed the camera was gone it disconnected and dropped back to Free, which is the borrowed-tier contract working correctly. The residue is a transient window where the UI claims Connected + Pro before the first failed heartbeat. Low severity and self-healing, but it is the kind of state that makes a real bug hard to read later. Worth showing 'Reconnecting…' rather than 'Connected' until the handshake completes. Not filed as a bug pending Eric's interest.

Caught immediately by the compiler. Repaired by locating both copies via content anchors and removing the duplicate; all markers back to a single occurrence and the APK rebuilt. No work lost. The lesson is the one already in the harness README: never anchor an edit on a string that repeats

The 3x-scale defect IS in the submitted build — but it is harmlessiOSINFOEric asked whether the render-scale bug is in 1.2.237 (build 675), now in App Store review. Yes: the UIGraphicsImageRenderer-without-format pattern is pre-existing code, not something the port introduced, and 675 was archived before CardRenderer existed. Impact measured rather than guessed: the 3840x2160 JPEG it produced is 372KB against YouTube's 2,097,152-byte limit — 5x more bytes than needed, comfortably accepted. The reel intro was never affected either: encodeImageToVideo draws the CGImage into a fixed 1920x1080 pixel buffer, so an oversized image just downscales. The outro never used that API at all (raw CGContext at explicit dimensions). Verdict: wasteful, not broken — no reason to pull the build from review
measured via CardRenderTests

Swapping the build would restart review AND put those fixes behind code that has never run. Build 676 with all four card surfaces is installed on his SE for device verification first

README lesson 27
Baseball per-player H vs team hits — RECONCILESAndroidPASSEric asked for a clean re-test after a messy input sequence left it ambiguous. Done as a unit test rather than one careful UI run, because a UI run proves one sequence while this proves the invariant. Both totals come from different code over the same source: recordAtBat bumps HalfInningRecord.hits on resultIsHit and the box score sums those half-innings, while getBatterGameStats re-counts at-bats with the same predicate. Verified they agree over a controlled sequence, that home hits don't leak into away, and that walks, HBP, sacrifices, errors, fielder's choice, double plays, catcher's interference and infield flies all count as non-hits
app/src/test/.../BaseballHitsReconcileTest.kt
The ONE way those two numbers can legitimately disagreeAndroidINFOAn at-bat whose batterIndex falls outside the displayed lineup counts toward the team total but appears on no batting line — so the box score would show more team H than the lines add up to. Pinned in a test rather than asserted away. Whether this is reachable depends on what index a substitute gets and whether the box score renders lines beyond the starting nine — worth Eric confirming, since it is the shape of the discrepancy he originally thought he saw
My own test arithmetic was wrong first timeAndroidINFOThe first run failed on my expected value (I said 5 hits for a sequence containing 4), not on the app. Worth recording because it is the failure mode of writing an expectation and a subject together: the test was right to fail and the product was never wrong
Emulator recording: real progress, still blocked — but now on ONE lineAndroidFAILThe AVD was running ANGLE-over-SwiftShader (software Vulkan). Relaunched with `-gpu host` and it now reports 'Google (Apple), Apple M4, 4.1 Metal'. The camera stack initialises where it previously did nothing — cameraserver and GCH_CameraDeviceSession come up. The failure moved to a single specific call: eglCreateWindowSurface fails in CameraEngine's render thread. NOTE eglChooseConfig SUCCEEDS first, so a config is returned and then rejected for a window surface. Prime suspect: EGLManager.chooseEglConfig asks for EGL_SURFACE_TYPE = WINDOW_BIT|PBUFFER_BIT in a single config — fine on real hardware, scarce on ANGLE. NOT changed: the EGL config governs recording on every real phone, and loosening it to please an emulator is not a change to make unattended
EGLManager.kt:48-66
Emulator relaunch recipe, for next timeAndroidINFO`adb emu kill`, remove the stale ~/.android/avd/<AVD>.avd/multiinstance.lock (the kill leaves it behind and the next launch dies with 'Running multiple emulators with the same AVD'), then `emulator -avd SF_Samsung_S26_Ultra -gpu host -no-snapshot-load -no-boot-anim`. Confirm with `adb shell dumpsys SurfaceFlinger | grep GLES:` — you want 'Apple M4 / Metal', not SwiftShader. Landscape is required before the app will record: `settings put system accelerometer_rotation 0` then `user_rotation 1`

Baseball H investigation (2026-08-20) 1 pass · 1 info

TestPlatformDetail
The real bug behind the H question: substitutes inherit the starter's at-batsAndroidPASSChased Eric's question to ground instead of leaving it as a hypothetical. substituteBatter replaces the lineup entry IN PLACE and moves the starter to benchHistory, so both players share one batting slot and every at-bat in it carries the same batterIndex. The exported scorebook grouped by slot against the CURRENT lineup, so the starter's at-bats printed under the substitute's name and the starter vanished from the box score. Team R/H/E was always correct — only attribution was wrong, which is precisely what makes it look like a totals bug. Fixed: at-bats are claimed by name+number first, slot only as fallback, and a subbed-out starter keeps their own line
BaseballScorebookExporter ~330
CLOSED: the hypothetical divergence I flagged cannot happenAndroidINFOLast night I flagged that an at-bat with a batterIndex outside the displayed lineup would count toward team H but show on no line, and asked Eric to confirm whether substitutes could cause it. Answered by reading the code rather than asking again: substitutes reuse the starter's battingPosition and the lineup never grows past nine, so the index is always in range and the totals always reconcile. That question is closed — but chasing it is what surfaced the attribution bug above
BaseballFullGame.substituteBatter

Emulator recording BREAKTHROUGH (2026-08-20) 2 pass · 2 info

TestPlatformDetail
Recording works on the emulator — the never-exercised branch is now testableAndroidPASSRoot cause was not the emulator: EGLManager asked for EGL_SURFACE_TYPE = WINDOW_BIT|PBUFFER_BIT, but the class only ever calls eglCreateWindowSurface and NOTHING in the app calls eglCreatePbufferSurface. It was requiring a capability the app never uses. On phones such configs are plentiful so it never showed; on the emulator's ANGLE driver they are scarce — eglChooseConfig returned one that could not back a window, and every recording died at eglCreateWindowSurface. Asking only for WINDOW fixes it. Eric offered a debug-only variant; not needed, because this was a wrong requirement rather than a workaround
EGLManager.chooseEglConfig

FIXED: a parked game is saved as the sport it was played asAndroidPASSRoot cause read from the code rather than guessed: buildGameSummary trusted the live `scoreboardMode` and only consulted the session when the live mode had degraded to GENERIC. scoreboardMode drifts — a controller mirrors the camera's sport — so the parked volleyball game saved as BASKETBALL. Inverted: the SESSION's sport now wins, falling back to the live mode only when there is no session or the session is GENERIC. Safe because a sport change calls createSession(sport = newMode) rather than mutating the current session, so a session's sport is by construction what its game was played as. A disagreement is now logged rather than silently resolved. Five unit tests pin the rule, including the exact rig case

FIXED: the controller no longer inherits a ghost gameAndroidPASSTraced with the project's own RemoteRoleGuard, which has been logging this since July and named the symptom exactly: 'this is how a ghost resume game card appears'. Its stack trace pointed at the culprit — the controller's Game Setup completion callback (MainActivity:1318) calling saveStateToPreferences(). The guard only REPORTED the write; the write still landed, and ScoreFrameState outlives the connection. Once disconnected isNonOwningController goes false, no session is found, and recoverStrandedSession() faithfully rebuilds one from the leftover — the ghost card, and the route to saving a game on the wrong phone. Fix: added shouldBlockLocalGameWrite so the write is BLOCKED rather than narrated. VERIFIED on the rig: ran the exact planting sequence, guard logged BLOCKED, and afterwards active_game_session=0, no resume card, history 0 — where before there was a session, a 'Resume Volleyball' card and a saved record

But it was deliberately non-fatal and purely informational, so the bad write proceeded anyway and the only detector remained Eric in a gym. A guard that can name a violation that precisely can usually PREVENT it — worth revisiting the other checkLocalGameWrite call sites on the same principle

NARROWED: the failure is specific to the game-start path, not web scoring itselfWeb/AndroidFAILSo the bug is real but much smaller than I reported: a scorer sees the game when it is RESUMED, and hangs on 'Waiting for game to start…' when the game is started fresh through Record → sport → setup. BUG: the web scorer keeps showing a game that has ENDEDWeb/AndroidFAILEnded the Generic game on the phone — it saved, and the phone moved to the game-detail page. The browser still displays that game as live: 'Generic / HOME 0 / PERIOD 1 / AWAY 0 / SCORE', with its scoring controls available. So a web scorekeeper can sit there scoring a match that finished, sending commands for a game the phone no longer has open. The end of a game is never published to the relay — the same missing-publish shape as the start, at the other end of the lifecycle
The publish happens on ACTIVITY CREATE, which is why it is so patchyWeb/AndroidINFOThe instrumentation makes the pattern clear: 'relay wiring reached' fires from MainActivity.onCreate, so the web is only ever updated when the game screen is CREATED — a resume from Home, a cold start. It did not fire when a game was started from an already-running MainActivity, and it cannot fire at all when a game ENDS, because the activity is not recreated. That single placement explains every symptom seen today: works on resume, hangs on start, and never clears on end. The fix is to publish on game lifecycle transitions rather than on activity creation

remote/protocol 1 pass · 2 bug · 1 info

TestPlatformDetail
iOS state syncs rejected by Android camera as stale (seq convention mismatch)ios-controller → android-cameraBUGAndroid controller sends FULL_STATE_SYNC seq = remoteScoringClient.lastServerSeq (the camera's state version). iOS sends seq = its own monotonic commandSeq from stamped() in RemoteScoringClient.swift:517. Camera rejects when cmd.seq < serverSeq (MainActivity.processFullStateSyncCommand). serverSeq climbs on every broadcastState; iOS's counter climbs only per command, so it falls behind within seconds and EVERY iOS score/stat update is silently dropped. End Game / Start Recording skip that check, which is why they still work. Matches Eric's 2026-08-01 report quoted in the code comment at MainActivity.kt:40307.
logcat: 'Rejecting stale FullStateSync: controller seq=1, server seq=32' — proven with drivers/wirectl.py --seq-mode command
CORRECTION + real root cause: FULL_STATE_SYNC stale gate compares two unrelated countersallBUGSupersedes my earlier 'iOS vs Android seq convention' entry — that was WRONG. Android's stampCommand (RemoteScoringClient.kt:565) overwrites seq with commandSeq.incrementAndGet() for FULL_STATE_SYNC, exactly as iOS's stamped() does. So the `seq = remoteScoringClient.lastServerSeq` computed at MainActivity.kt:4112 NEVER REACHES THE WIRE — it is dead code, overwritten downstream. The camera then gates on `cmd.seq < serverSeq` (processFullStateSyncCommand), comparing the CONTROLLER'S per-command counter against the CAMERA'S state-broadcast counter. serverSeq increments on every broadcastState (very frequent); the controller's counter increments only per command sent. The camera leads almost immediately, so FULL_STATE_SYNC gets rejected as 'stale' for ANY controller — Android too, not just iOS. It only appears to work early in a session while the counters are still close. Recommended fix (needs Eric's OK — protocol semantics): either drop the stale gate and rely on CommandSeqTracker for dedupe, or carry the intended value in a NEW optional field (e.g. baseSeq) that the stamping choke point doesn't touch, and compare that. Additive field = protocol-safe per CLAUDE.md.
logcat 'Rejecting stale FullStateSync: controller seq=1, server seq=32'; same sync with seq=serverSeq applied cleanly.
Evidence for BUG B: the iOS camera has NO stale gate, and cross-platform scoring works because of itallINFODirectly relevant to Eric's pending decision on BUG B. Android's camera rejects FULL_STATE_SYNC when `cmd.seq < serverSeq` — two unrelated counters — which is why state syncs get dropped. The iOS camera has no equivalent check (RemoteScoringServer.swift has the csid-based replay dedupe that mirrors Android's CommandSeqTracker, but no seq-vs-serverSeq stale rejection). Empirically the iOS camera applied an Android controller's volleyball scoring perfectly — 3-2, sets, serve — with no stale gate in the path. That is working evidence for BUG B fix option 1 (drop the gate and rely on CommandSeqTracker for dedupe): one platform already ships that way and behaves correctly.
BUG B FIXED + verified: stale gate removed, low-seq syncs now applyandroid-cameraPASSEric chose 'drop the gate'. Removed the cmd.seq < serverSeq check in processFullStateSyncCommand. Verified with the exact case that used to fail: a stamped sync carrying the controller's low per-command counter. Before: 'Rejecting stale FullStateSync: controller seq=1, server seq=32', scoreboard unchanged. After: 'FullStateSync APPLIED', scoreboard went to 6-2. Replay dedupe is untouched — CommandSeqTracker still drops duplicates at the socket layer before this code runs.
fixed: v1.1.1297

Root cause: a bowling payload can carry fewer than 10 frames (or none), but findCurrentFrame walks 0..9 unconditionally. NOTE: my Bug C sweep did not close this — it only converted `json.get*`, so the inner frame-object getters (fObj/gObj/bObj) were still bare, AND no amount of getter-hardening fixes an index-out-of-bounds downstream. FIX (v1.1.1299): pad the frame list to 10 at the one place it's constructed, so every consumer is safe; convert the 15 inner-object getters the first sweep missed; and guard findCurrentFrame itself. VERIFIED with the exact payload that crashed 1298 (bowling, no frames array): renders 'E2E Bowler / 178 / Series 530 / Max 300 / Frame 1 · Roll 1' — series total correct at 352+178 — zero crashes, app alive.

TRAP 59 -- grep wrapper returns SILENCE on binary-ish files, faked a deploy alarmharnessBUG`grep` in this shell is a shell function from the claude shell snapshot that execs a ugrep-flavoured binary with `-I` (skip binary files). A file with one stray non-UTF8 byte is 'binary', and grep then prints NOTHING and exits 1 -- no warning, no 'Binary file matches'. Indistinguishable from a real zero-result search. functions/index.js (8,004 lines) is such a file: `grep -n 'require(' index.js` returned nothing while `head -25` showed requires plainly, and `grep -c 'require'` printed nothing at all -- not even '0', which is the tell, since a working grep always prints a count for -c. COST: I concluded index.js never requires shrink_guard_trigger.js and therefore that the next firebase deploy would DELETE 10 live data-protection triggers. Entirely manufactured by the tool -- index.js:6384 does require it. Caught by re-reading with head/sed instead of trusting the silence. FIX: `command grep -an` (-a = binary as text, `command` = bypass the shell function) for anything under functions/, or use the dedicated Grep tool, which has no such failure mode. Written up as trap 59. Third instance this run of the same shape (see traps 47, 54): an empty search result is evidence about the SEARCH, not about the CODE.

TRAP 60 -- the finalized Android recording lands in DCIM/ScoreFrame, not Movies/ScoreFrameandroidINFOWhile recording, the file exists as /sdcard/Movies/ScoreFrame/.pending-<n>-SF_*.mp4 (MediaStore IS_PENDING=1, direct-to-MediaStore via FileDescriptor). Watching only Movies/ScoreFrame therefore shows a file that grows and then VANISHES, which reads exactly like the recording was discarded. It was not. TRAP 61 -- 'newest recording' by FILENAME picks a 12-day-old fileandroidBUGNear-miss caught before it entered the record. Android recordings are named SF_<sport>_<randomhex>_<YYYYMMDD>_<HHMM>_<nnn>.mp4 -- the RANDOM HEX sits before the date, so sorting filenames lexicographically sorts by a random number, not by time. Picking max() by name after today's volleyball recording returned SF_VB_f5d6da61_20260812_1227_001.mp4, a file from 2026-08-12. Because it was also a volleyball file, its frame showed a perfectly plausible volleyball board -- I would have filed a 12-day-old frame as today's evidence and never noticed. This is the same class as trap 58 (stale iOS file reported as success), and the reason it is dangerous is that the wrong answer LOOKS right. FIX, both applied in android_burnin.py: sort by the MediaStore's date_added column, not the name; and hard-fail if the newest finalized recording is more than 600s old, so the harness refuses to file a stale frame instead of quietly succeeding.

SIGSTOP makes the label oscillate — rig artifact, not a product bugharnessINFODuring the frozen-camera run the bar alternated Reconnecting… / Connected (Wi-Fi) roughly every 4-5s rather than staying stale. Worth writing down so nobody files it. CAUSE: SIGSTOP freezes the fake camera's PROCESS but leaves its listening socket open, and the kernel keeps completing TCP handshakes into the backlog. So after the 4-beat timeout the client's auto-reconnect CONNECTS SUCCESSFULLY, resets its counters and repaints 'Connected', then goes stale again 8s later. The loop is the rig, not the app. A real Wi-Fi drop or a powered-off camera phone refuses the connection, so the client would stay in the reconnect state instead of cycling. If a future test needs a faithful partition, drop the packets rather than freezing the process -- kill the listener entirely, or block the port -- so connect() fails the way it would in a gym.
emulator run t=2.6/16.9/26.3/35.1s Reconnecting…, t=7.1/12.5/21.7/30.7s Connected
TRAP 66 -- re-resolve the sim container after every install; the UUID movesharnessBUG`xcrun simctl get_app_container <udid> <bundle> data` can return a DIFFERENT path after a reinstall. Caching it and writing fixtures to the old path fails SILENTLY -- the writes succeed, the directory still exists, and the app simply never sees them. Cost: I concluded a product bug (rosters not resolving, per-player stats silently missing) that did not exist, and wrote it into RESUME as something to chase. RULE: re-resolve the container immediately before any fixture write, never reuse a path across an install, and CONFIRM THE APP AGREES -- read the value back through the UI (here, the Teams tab's roster count) before trusting a seeded fixture. A fixture that the app cannot see looks exactly like a feature that does not work. Same family as traps 59/61/64/65: the tool answered confidently about something other than what I was asking.
0EF7A4CD-662E-4850-8F38-B51D9F2627B5 -> 41A62E2C-1DE0-4726-97F5-47A16B415B22
TRAP 67 -- a driver whose body runs at IMPORT drove the device mid-testharnessBUGios_editroster_sweep.py had its run loop at module level. Importing it to borrow tap_scrolled() and leave_game() therefore RELAUNCHED THE APP AND DROVE NINE GAMES as a side effect. Twice it left the simulator in a soccer game while I was mid-way through testing basketball, and I read the resulting screen as a basketball result -- once reporting 'no roster chips' from a soccer panel. Fixed: the body now lives in main() under an `if __name__ == '__main__'` guard, so importing is inert. Rule for this harness: a driver module must never move the device on import.

remote/robustness 1 pass · 1 bug

A FULL_STATE_SYNC missing one field CRASHES the camera app (FATAL EXCEPTION)android-cameraBUGdeserializeState's SOCCER branch uses bare json.getInt/getString for most fields. A sync omitting one (I omitted homeCorners) throws an uncaught JSONException on the main thread and kills the camera process mid-game — logcat 'FATAL EXCEPTION: main ... No value for homeCorners', PID 27884 died and restarted as 29214. Recording would die with it. SCOPE, checked: not a live production crash today — iOS's SoccerState.toJson and the LIVE web scorer (fetched from scoreframe.app/score.html) both send every field Android requires. But it violates the protocol convention CLAUDE.md states explicitly ('All new fields use opt*() with defaults'), so any version skew where a controller drops a field takes the camera down. Football's equivalent arrays ARE guarded with json.has() — soccer just wasn't. Recommend an opt*()-with-default sweep across every sport's deserialize branch.
logcat FATAL EXCEPTION: main / JSONException: No value for homeCorners; process pid changed 27884 -> 29214
BUG C FIXED + verified: a partial state sync no longer crashes the cameraandroid-cameraPASSEric chose the full sweep. Converted all 93 bare json.getInt/getString/getBoolean calls in deserializeState to opt*() with defaults, across all 12 sports, and made 28 getJSONArray reads null-safe. Defaults were taken from existing opt* precedents in the same file where they existed, otherwise from the ScoreboardType data-class defaults (useQuarters=true, isTopOfInning=true, currentHalf/Inning=1, down=1, distance=10, possession/serving='HOME') — not invented. ALSO FOUND during the sweep: basketball's homeScoreByPeriod/awayScoreByPeriod and bowling's frames/previousGames were unguarded too, so they would have crashed the same way soccer did. After: applies cleanly, scoreboard shows 3-1, app pid alive.

Controller-owns mode removed — and it was NOT as dormant as I reportedandroidPASSEric chose 'delete it'. IMPORTANT CORRECTION to what I told him: I said nothing sets it. The owner-picker dialog was indeed dead code (HomeActivity had a comment saying so, and startRemoteGame hard-sets CAMERA) — but THREE ingest points parsed it straight off the WIRE: MainActivity's lifecycle handler and RemoteScoringClient in two places, each doing GameOwner.valueOf(gameOwnerStr). A peer sending gameOwner:'CONTROLLER' could therefore still flip an Android phone into controller-owns mode. That is exactly what made 'the game never saves on the controller' conditional. Fix: ConnectionManager.gameOwner is now a property whose setter refuses anything but CAMERA and logs the attempt, so all six write paths including the three wire ones are covered. Deleted the 88-line dead dialog. The enum member is kept so the ~18 existing == CONTROLLER checks still compile and stay false; removing it touches 18 branches and deserves its own reviewed pass.
fixed: v1.1.1297

remote/ui 1 info

TestPlatformDetail
iOS controller wrong-sport panel: root cause found in code, NOT reproduced tonightios-controllerINFOControllerScreen.scoringPanelScrollView switches on `sport`, which comes from `client.connectedSport` (line 161). RemoteScoringClient sets connectedSport ONLY on connectionAck, stateUpdate and fullStateSync — never on gameLifecycle. startRemoteGame(with:) (line 892) sends gameLifecycle(START, sport:) and never sets it. So between sending START and the camera's first state echo, the controller renders the PREVIOUS sport's panel; if the echo is slow or lost it stays wrong. That matches the original report exactly (started volleyball, controller stayed on a basketball panel). HONEST CAVEAT: I could NOT reproduce it tonight — ending a game bounces the iOS controller back to Home, which resets the flow, so the two games never ran back-to-back in one controller session. Fix would be to set connectedSport optimistically in startRemoteGame/startRemoteBowlingGame and let the camera's echo correct it. Not applied: unreproduced, and it is iOS code touching remote state.

web-scoring 1 pass · 2 bug · 1 info

TestPlatformDetail
Web scoring NOT fixed — but the real root cause is now identifiedandroid-cameraBUGMy earlier diagnosis ('the publish is wired to MainActivity.onCreate') was incomplete. I added the missing piece — FirebaseRelayServer.publishGameState(), called from ConnectionManager.setGameState so every lifecycle transition writes to Firebase instead of only setting a field nothing wrote. That change is correct and shipped, but it is NOT sufficient and web scoring still fails. THE ACTUAL ROOT CAUSE: setGameState is never CALLED when a game starts in a web-scoring session. Reproduced on 1.1.1297: hosted a session, joined from a real browser, started a Basketball game, ended a Soccer game — logcat shows ZERO 'Game state:' transitions throughout, and the browser stayed on 'Waiting for game to start…' the entire time. role IS Role.SERVER (set in startInternetServer), so the gate isn't the issue. The lifecycle simply doesn't notify the relay: setGameState(ACTIVE) lives in MainActivity's remote-server setup block, which a plain local game with a relay attached never enters. There's a v1.1.355 comment in startInternetServer that already half-knew this — it carries gameState forward at session creation so a session started MID-game publishes ACTIVE, which only papers over the case where the game already exists. NEXT: call setGameState on the ordinary game start/end path, not just the remote-server one.
logcat: no 'Game state:' lines across a full start and a full end; browser unchanged
CORRECTION 3 — Android publishes correctly. The bug is in score.html, and it is narrow.webBUGSupersedes my two earlier diagnoses, both of which blamed Android. They were wrong. PROVEN CORRECT ON ANDROID: read the live RTDB with the service account while a game was running — sessions/<code>/state held gameState=ACTIVE, sport=HOCKEY, a fully populated stateJson, and scorerRequest.status=approved. logcat showed 'Game state: IDLE → ACTIVE (sport=HOCKEY)' and 'Firebase relay wired for web scoring'. The camera does everything right. THE ACTUAL BUG: score.html does not act on the state that ALREADY EXISTS when it joins — it only reacts to a LATER change. Held a browser open across a score change and watched it flip: t+5s and t+10s 'waitingForGameScreen', then at t+15s (right after I scored on the camera) 'scoringScreen' with correct live data — HOME 3, AWAY 1, SOG/PIM, the goal buttons, everything. LIKELY CAUSE (code read, not yet proven): a race between the two listeners registered in join(). listenForApproval fires on status=approved and, because currentState/gameState aren't populated yet, calls showWaitingForGame() — clobbering the scoring screen listenForState may have just entered. Any subsequent state write re-enters scoring, which is exactly the observed behaviour. WHY THIS LOOKED LIKE 'never works': every check I ran opened a FRESH browser, so I was always testing 'join an already-active session' — the one case that fails. A scorer who joins BEFORE the game starts is fine; one who joins a game in progress sits on 'Waiting for game to start…' until the next score.
RTDB: gameState=ACTIVE/sport=HOCKEY/approved; held-open page: waiting → scoringScreen on first state change
The Android relay publish added in 1297 is correct but was not the fixandroid-cameraINFOFirebaseRelayServer.publishGameState() + the setGameState hook stay in — they make every lifecycle transition write to Firebase instead of only setting a field nothing wrote, which is right on its own merits and harmless. But they were not what was broken: the existing relay-wiring block in MainActivity (the one with the SFWEB diagnostic) already calls setGameState(ACTIVE) and broadcastState for ordinary game launches, and it fires correctly. My 'setGameState is never called' claim came from a contaminated run — a leftover Soccer game meant I exercised the RESUME path, not a fresh start.
FIXED + verified: joining a game already in progress now works (stale meta.sport)android-camera + webPASSPROVED the clobber with a MutationObserver on the live page: the state listener correctly entered scoringScreen at 3592ms, and 44ms later something knocked it back to waitingForGameScreen. ROOT CAUSE — a stale field on the ANDROID side, presenting as a web bug: the relay writes the `meta` node ONCE in createSession and never refreshes it, so meta.sport kept whatever sport existed when the session was made ('GENERIC' for a session created before the game started). meta/status and meta/lastSeenAt are updated later; meta/sport never was. state.sport meanwhile tracked correctly. score.html trusts meta over state and on mismatch does `currentState = null` — wiping the good state it had just applied. FIX (v1.1.1298, Android only — no live web edit needed): writeState now keeps meta/sport in step with state/sport, writing only when it actually changes. VERIFIED: fresh session, Volleyball started BEFORE anyone joined (the case that always failed). meta.sport=VOLLEYBALL == state.sport=VOLLEYBALL. Timeline: ENTER scoringScreen at 3478ms, NO clobber, final active screen = scoringScreen, and the page renders the real volleyball panel including the per-player stat buttons (AST / D / B / ERR / MSV).

ios/remote 7 pass · 1 bug

TestPlatformDetail
REPRODUCED + FIXED: iOS controller showed the previous game's sport panelios-camera/ios-controllerPASSEric asked me to wait until it reproduced. It did, on the second game of a controller session: picked Hockey, the camera went to 🏒 Hockey, and the controller sat on a 🏀 Basketball panel. NOT COSMETIC — the mounted panel's onChange ships a fullStateSync stamped with ITS sport, so scoring on the stale panel would have switched the camera's sport mid-game. Cause: the panel is chosen from client.connectedSport, which is only set from connectionAck/stateUpdate/fullStateSync — never from a lifecycle START. My FIRST attempt (set connectedSport optimistically) FAILED, twice: the camera's first echo after START still carries its PREVIOUS sport and overwrote it. My SECOND attempt added an `intendedSport` state that wins until the camera agrees — but it ALSO failed, because writing connectedSport fired the very onChange that clears intendedSport (echoed == want), wiping it instantly. Final fix: intendedSport ONLY, no optimistic write to connectedSport; cleared when the camera's echo agrees and on end-game. VALIDATED across two back-to-back games in one session: game 1 Basketball on both, game 2 Hockey on BOTH the controller and the camera.
FIXED: ENDING GAME sheet auto-dismisses instead of blocking the lobbyiosPASSThe sheet stayed up after every step was ✓, sitting over the lobby and silently eating taps — three 'Start a Game' taps in a row reported success and did nothing. Now auto-dismisses 1.8s after all steps are terminal. First version only handled allDone CHANGING, which missed the case that actually bit: the camera relaunching re-sends its end-game mirror and re-activates the store while allDone is ALREADY true, so onChange never fires — verified it stayed stuck. Added an onAppear check too. Re-verified: sheet gone by t+4s including across a camera restart.
fixed: auto-dismiss in EndGameStatusBox
FIXED: controller offered to create a LOCAL team while the camera owns teamsios-controllerPASSPicking a sport on the controller popped 'Create a Hockey team?' plus an in-form 'Create Hockey Team' card. On a controller the CAMERA owns teams, so a team made here lands on the wrong phone and isn't the team the game files under. Gated both the alert and the card on `teamsOverride == nil`, which is exactly the signal the controller passes the camera's teams through. Verified: setup now goes straight to Home Team / Away Team with no create offer.
OPEN BUG: controller cannot reconnect after the camera app restartsios-camera/ios-controllerBUGAfter the camera app restarts, the controller sees it in Nearby Cameras and resolves the address, but every connect attempt fails with 'Connecting… / Waiting: The operation couldn't be completed'. Repeatable. Restarting the CONTROLLER app clears it immediately and the same tap connects first time, so the controller is holding unusable connection state rather than the camera refusing. RULED OUT: an adb forward holding 127.0.0.1:9876 caused an identical-looking failure earlier (my own harness interfering with the sim's bind) — this reproduces with all forwards removed and the camera confirmed listening on *:9876. Not fixed.
FIXED + verified: controller can reconnect after the camera app restartsios-controllerPASSRoot cause: NWConnection enters .waiting when the endpoint is momentarily unreachable — exactly what a camera restart looks like — and then retries FOREVER. The .waiting branch only set lastError; it never changed connectionState (stuck on .connecting) and never cancelled the connection. connect() then early-returned on `guard connectionState == .disconnected || .reconnecting`, so every subsequent tap was a SILENT no-op. Only relaunching the controller app cleared it. Two-part fix: (1) a 6s watchdog on .waiting cancels the stalled connection and returns to .disconnected with 'Couldn't reach the camera. Tap to try again.'; (2) an explicit connect() now supersedes a stalled .connecting attempt instead of no-oping — a user tapping a camera must always mean 'try now'. Still refuses while genuinely connected/handshaking. VERIFIED on the exact scenario: paired, restarted the CAMERA app, then reconnected from the controller WITHOUT relaunching it — 'Connected to Camera'. Previously impossible.
fixed: iOS build 680
FIXED: phones no longer disagree about the connection after End Gameios-camera/ios-controllerPASSEnding a game sent the controller to app HOME, which renders no connection state at all — so the camera still showed 'Controller connected!' while the controller looked completely disconnected, and starting a second game meant going back through Remote → I'm the Controller. Fix: when the end-game box closes, if the client is STILL connected, route to the controller LOBBY (.controller mode) instead of .home. The lobby shows 'Connected to Camera' and offers 'Start a Game', which is what the user wants next. If not connected — the normal camera-side end — it still goes Home. VERIFIED: after End Game the controller shows 'Connected to Camera / iOS / No game in progress yet' and the camera shows 'Connected to Controller Phone' — they agree. NEGATIVE TESTS PASS: both kill directions on iOS↔iOSios-camera/ios-controllerPASSKILL A — controller dies mid-game: killed the controller app during a live Volleyball game. The camera kept the game, kept the 'End' button (correctly reclaiming End Game control per Eric's rule 'End Game only on the controller, UNLESS it disconnects'), and showed 'Listening for Wi-Fi / Bluetooth…' ready for a rejoin. FOUND + FIXED by the kill test: reconnecting mid-game showed the WRONG sportios-camera/ios-controllerPASSSurfaced while running kill test A. After killing the controller during a Volleyball game and rejoining, the FRESHLY RELAUNCHED controller rendered a 🏀 Basketball panel while the camera ran 🏐 Volleyball. A different path from the earlier wrong-sport bug — no START involved, so the intendedSport fix didn't cover it. A reconnecting controller takes its sport from CONNECTION_ACK, and the ACK reports the server's cached `currentSport`, which is only refreshed in start() and sendStateSync(). A controller-initiated START switches the camera through a DIFFERENT path, so the cache kept the value from before the game — and it initialises to 'BASKETBALL', which is exactly what the controller showed. Fix: (1) a `liveSportProvider` closure so the ACK reads the camera's live sport instead of a cache that can go stale; (2) `updateCurrentSport()` called on the game-setup path as well. My first attempt only patched the fullStateSync branch and FAILED — that path never runs for a controller-initiated start. VERIFIED: kill + rejoin mid-game now shows 🏐 Volleyball on both.
fixed: iOS build 684

iOS GENERIC remote state never reached the scoring panel — found by the iOS matrix, FIXEDios-cameraPASSThis is exactly the bug the iOS coverage gap was hiding, and the mirror of Android's 1296. In ContentView's fullStateSync handler every sport has an explicit branch except GENERIC, which falls into the final `else`. That else sets `genericState` (the raw dict driving the burned-in overlay) and calls updateScoreboardGeneric() — but never sets `genericViewModel.state`, which is what the SCORING PANEL reads. So a Generic game scored from a controller left the camera's panel on 0–0 while all 11 other sports applied correctly. Persistent, not lag: confirmed across two sends and 16+ seconds. Fix: also set genericViewModel.state = GenericState.fromJson(stateJson), mirroring what the restore-from-disk path already does. VERIFIED: camera now shows HOME 21 — 13 AWAY.

Web scoring 11 pass · 6 bug · 6 info

TestPlatformDetail
A web scorer joining mid-game WIPED OUT the scoreAndroid camera + webBUGData loss, reproduced cold and deterministically. Camera at 2-0 Basketball; a fresh web scorer joined, its page rendered 0-0 rather than the live game, and the moment it tapped +1 the camera read 1-0. The 2 points were simply gone. Repeated it: camera 1-0, joiner shows 0-0, taps +3, camera reads 3-0 - the live score replaced wholesale by a blank page plus one tap. Cause: the camera only wrote game state to the relay while a scorer was ALREADY connected (an isClientConnected() gate), so the relay kept the empty state it was born with at session creation. Anyone joining a game in progress read that stale blank and their first full-state sync overwrote the real game with it. This is exactly the failure mode of "share the link with a parent in the stands" - the feature the relay exists for.
fixed: v1.1.1301 - the camera keeps the relay copy current for as long as the session exists, audience or not, and publishes the live state the instant a scorer is approved.
After: client A scores to 3-0, a second scorer joins and its page shows 3-0, taps +2, camera reads 5-0. Score accumulates instead of being replaced.

Bowling scored from the web never reaches the cameraAndroid camera + webBUGThe only sport of the twelve where web scoring does not work. Threw three strikes from the browser in a single session: the page tracked them correctly and showed a frame total of 30. The Android camera stayed on Bowler 1, total 0, Series 0, Frame 1 Roll 1 - nothing applied. Not a transport problem: logcat shows the command arriving with frames[0].roll1="X" and the camera logging "FullStateSync APPLIED sport=BOWLING", and applyRemoteState does assign bowlingScore = state. So the state is received, deserialized and assigned, and then does not survive to the panel or to the state the camera publishes back - a fresh web scorer re-seeds to an empty game every time, which is how I first noticed. Suspect the bowling panel derives its frame/roll from activity-level editing fields written by LOCAL input rather than from bowlingScore, so a remote sync updates the model and nothing reads it.
Web: 3 strikes, total 30. Camera at the same moment: 0, Series 0, Frame 1 Roll 1. Repeated across separate sessions.
NOT FIXED tonight - deliberatelyAndroidINFOI stopped short of fixing the bowling case rather than shipping a speculative change. The remaining unknown is which field the bowling panel actually reads, and bowling is the one area of this app that has already eaten real data (the frame-by-frame loss that led to the merge/shrink rules). A wrong guess there at 2:45am is worse than a clean handover, and I have already shipped two Android builds tonight. The evidence above is enough to fix it deliberately in daylight.
Bowling scored from the web never reaches the cameraAndroid camera + webBUGFIXED in v1.1.1302 - root cause found. Bowling is the only sport whose wire payload carries the active bowler TWICE: a top-level frames array (kept for older senders) and a bowlers[] array. For an Android sender the two are built from the same list and always agree, so which one the deserializer read never mattered. The web scorer broke that symmetry: score.html seeds its state from whatever the camera published - INCLUDING Android bowlers[] - then scores into the top-level frames only and posts the whole object back, stale bowlers[] and all. The deserializer preferred bowlers[], so every roll thrown from a browser was silently discarded while the camera logged the sync as applied. iOS never sends bowlers[] at all, which is why only this combination failed.
fixed: v1.1.1302 - top-level frames win for the ACTIVE bowler when present. No-op for Android (identical by construction) and for iOS (never sends bowlers[]); other bowlers keep their own frames.
Three strikes from the browser: page reads 30, camera reads 30, Series 30, Frame 4 Roll 1 - correct bowling scoring. Before the fix the camera sat on Frame 1 Roll 1 with 0. Confirmed with a temporary in-app diagnostic (since removed) that the state arrived EMPTY from deserialization, not from the UI.
Bowling - web scoring end to endAndroid camera + webPASSThree strikes driven from the browser land on the Android camera with correct bowling maths (frame 1 = 30) and the panel advances to Frame 4 Roll 1. Twelfth of twelve sports.
Softball - web scoring end to endAndroid camera + webPASSBrowser rendered the Softball panel (its own emoji, same line score as baseball); pressed Run and the Android camera read AWAY 0 - 1 HOME. Twelve of twelve sports now drive the camera from the web page.
All 12 sports verified from the web pageAndroid camera + webINFOBasketball, Football, Soccer, Volleyball, Hockey, Lacrosse, Tennis, Pickleball, Baseball, Softball, Generic, Bowling - each joined by code, panel checked for that sport's own controls, scored from a real browser, and confirmed on the Android camera scoreboard. Started the night at 2 of 12.
RETRACTED: the Generic game that saved as 0-0AndroidINFODoes not reproduce, and I no longer think it happened. Drove it deliberately: Generic game, scored to 1-0 from the browser, ended on the camera - the saved game reads WON 1-0, FINAL HOME 1 AWAY 0. Flagging the retraction rather than leaving a scary open item on the board.
score.html now trusts the live game over the session record (score v6)WebPASSHardening, shipped to scoreframe.app. The page took its sport from meta.sport, which is written when a session is created and refreshed by the camera on a sport change - so a camera on an OLDER build never refreshes it, and the meta handler would then null out currentState on every meta update, throwing away the live scoreboard and stranding the scorer on "Waiting for game to start...". That is the bug I fixed from the sender side earlier tonight; this closes it for users whose camera phone has not updated. State from the camera is always fresher than the session note, so once state names the sport it wins and meta is only a pre-state fallback. The sport-change side effect (dropping the pinned stat player) moved to the state listener with it.
fixed: score v6 on Pages.
Validated on a LOCAL copy first against two live sessions (Volleyball 1->4, Basketball with correct sport + live seed), plus a page held open ~40s with no stranding. Then deployed and re-validated against the live page: +2 twice on the deployed score v6 -> camera reads HOME 12.
iOS: hosting a link mid-game stranded the scorer foreveriOS camera + webBUGFound by testing web scoring against an iOS camera for the first time. Start a game on iOS, tap the remote icon, Invite via Internet, share the link - the scorer joins and sits on "Waiting for game to start..." indefinitely while the game is running in front of them. TWO separate causes, both fixed. (1) createSession reset gameState to IDLE unconditionally, wiping the ACTIVE the mid-game host path set immediately before calling it - the reset was added for a good reason (a stale ENDED leaking into a new session) but it ignored the caller. (2) currentSportStateJson() deliberately returns an EMPTY dictionary, on the assumption that the next broadcast would fill it in - but that only fires when the score next CHANGES, so hosting and sharing without scoring left the session with no scoreboard at all. The page needs both an ACTIVE state and a payload. Firebase also drops empty objects, so the key was absent rather than blank. Hosting FIRST and then starting a game always worked, which is why this survived: that order sets ACTIVE again afterwards.
fixed: iOS 686 (createSession honours an initialGameState) + 687 (push the real scoreboard immediately after hosting).
Before: relay had gameState IDLE and no stateJson; page waited 40s+ without entering scoring. After 687: relay shows gameState ACTIVE and stateJson present, and the page drops straight into the bowling panel.
iOS: bowling scored from the web does not reach the cameraiOS camera + webBUGConfirmed, NOT fixed - and a DIFFERENT cause from the Android one I fixed in 1302. Three strikes from the browser: the page reads 30, the iOS camera stays on Frame 1 Roll 1, and the relay then goes back to empty frames because the camera republishes its own unchanged game over the top. Android's cause was the payload carrying the bowler twice and the deserializer preferring the stale copy; that cannot be it here, because the relay dump shows iOS publishes NO bowlers[] array at all (bowlers_len: null) - only top-level frames. So iOS is dropping the web's frames for some other reason. Next step is the same one that cracked Android: a temporary diagnostic printing what the iOS deserializer actually produces, rather than more reading.
iOS: bowling scored from the web does not reach the cameraiOS camera + webBUGFIXED in iOS 689 - and my diagnosis last round was WRONG in an instructive way. I reported this as a bowling-specific parsing problem and argued it could not share Android's cause. It was neither. The relay command delegate is wired once, when the camera starts, guarded on whether a relay exists AT THAT MOMENT. Host a session later - from inside a running game, which is the natural way to do it - and no delegate is ever attached. Commands from the web arrived at the relay and went nowhere. The scorer could see the game and score on their own page while the phone ignored every tap and republished its own state over the top, which is exactly what made it look like bowling was being dropped. It affected EVERY sport, not just bowling.
fixed: iOS 689 - attachRelayDelegate() on the mid-game host path.
A temporary NSLog in the bowling apply branch never fired at all on 688, which ruled out parsing and pointed at delivery. After 689 it fires with parsed f1r1=X total=30 and the iOS camera reads 30, Series 30.
CORRECTION: web scoring never worked against an iOS camera hosted mid-gameiOS camera + webINFOScope correction on the above. I framed this last round as a bowling problem; it was the whole feature. Any web scorer invited from inside a running game on iOS could watch the scoreboard and tap all they liked - nothing reached the phone, for any of the twelve sports. Hosting from Home BEFORE starting a game was the only path that worked, because that order happens to wire the delegate. Three separate bugs stacked on that same mid-game path tonight (686 game state, 687 missing scoreboard, 689 no delegate), which is a fair sign nobody had ever driven it end to end.
iOS per-sport web sweep - BLOCKED, and partly my own doingiOS camera + webINFOI wanted to repeat the 12-sport web sweep against an iOS camera now that the plumbing works. The simulator had used its 3 free Web Scoring sessions, so I reinstalled the app to reset the counter - which also signed it out, and hosting requires an account ("Sign in first (Account tab) to host a game"). I am not going to start typing Eric's credentials into a simulator at 6am, so the sweep stops here. Worth being clear that the block is self-inflicted: the sensible order would have been to check what a reinstall wipes before doing it.
Why the remaining iOS-web gap is narrowiOS camera + webINFONot a claim that the sweep is done - a note on how much is actually left. The web path has two halves and each is independently covered. (1) The iOS side that APPLIES an incoming state is the same code for the web scorer as for a controller phone - applyRemoteCommand into each sport's fromJson - and all 12 sports were verified against an iOS camera earlier tonight over the phone-to-phone path. (2) The web page's per-sport payloads were verified across all 12 sports against an Android camera. What was untested was the JOIN between them, and that is exactly what tonight's three iOS fixes addressed, verified end to end with bowling - the most complex payload of the twelve and the one that broke on Android for a different reason. So the residual risk is a sport whose payload the iOS parser handles differently from the Android one, which is a narrow target rather than an open field.
