Cleanups 2 pass · 1 info

TestPlatformDetail
Dead HockeyControllerPanel removediOSPASS116 lines defining a hockey scoring panel referenced from nowhere - hockey on the controller uses the shared panel like every other sport. Deleted; iOS still builds. No version bump, so it rides along with the next build.
ANSWERED: nothing ever sets the controller-owns-game modeAndroid + iOSINFOThis was an open question of Eric's. Checked both platforms: there is no assignment of gameOwner to CONTROLLER anywhere in the Android or the iOS source - only comparisons against it. So the whole controller-owns configuration is unreachable, and every branch that asks "unless the controller owns the game" is dead weight sitting on top of the most safety-critical rule in the app (never save on the controller). Worth deleting, but it touches ~18 branches around game saving, so it wants a deliberate pass in daylight rather than a 3am refactor - and it explains why every kill/save test tonight behaved so consistently.
Controller-owns mode removed from both platformsAndroid + iOSPASSEric approved deletion. Android: enum, property, wire parsing and every branch gone - the save rule is now one unconditional line (a controller never saves) instead of two mirrored conditions that depended on a value a peer could send. iOS: CORRECTION to what I reported earlier - this mode was NOT dead here. ControllerScreen presented a GameOwnerPickerView whenever a controller paired before a game was running, and choosing controller genuinely handed ownership over. That is why the rule was conditional and why the platforms disagreed. Picker removed, ownership claimed automatically, and the wire value pinned so an older peer cannot flip a camera out of owning its own game. Both platforms build clean.

Harness traps 1 bug · 16 info · 2 skip

TestPlatformDetail
The App Store rating prompt is INVISIBLE to idb - it looks like a wedged appiOS harnessINFOCost 10 minutes and nearly got logged as an iOS hang. After ending a game on the iOS camera, every accessibility dump returned a single label - "ScoreFrame" - and stayed that way through a ten-minute wait loop, while launchctl showed the app alive. It was not hung: StoreKit's "Enjoying ScoreFrame?" rating dialog was on screen, and being a system dialog it does not appear in idb's tree at all. A screenshot showed it instantly. RULE: when an iOS dump collapses to one label or looks blank, SCREENSHOT before concluding anything - the same trap as the keychain Save Password prompt already in the README.
One derivedDataPath per build filled the diskharnessINFOA build failed with "No space left on device", and then the tool could not even write its own output file. Cause was mine: three separate Release derived-data trees from passing a fresh -derivedDataPath to each xcodebuild run. Removing just those three freed about 16GB. Now using a single shared /tmp/sfdd for every iOS build. Worth knowing before deleting anything of Eric's - his Xcode DerivedData is 6.3GB but that is his working cache, not mine to clear.
A fresh iOS install is a seven-gate gauntlet before any test can runiOS harnessINFOReinstalling to reset a counter cost most of a round. In order: the notification permission sheet, five onboarding pages, the coach/fan role picker, a What's New sheet, a first-game coach mark, the create-a-team offer, the camera permission sheet - and then sign-in, which is the one that cannot be automated. iossportweb.py now handles all but the last. If a test needs a clean install, budget for that; if it only needs a counter reset, find a narrower way.
THE LANDSCAPE GOTCHA: idb taps address a different coordinate space than it reportsiOS harnessBUGThis is why iOS landscape automation looked impossible, and it was never an app problem. describe-all reports frames in the app's CURRENT orientation, but ui tap addresses the DEVICE-NATIVE portrait space. In landscape the two disagree, so every tap computed from a reported frame lands somewhere else entirely - silently. Top-row buttons did nothing at all, while a tap aimed at a score button opened an ad in Safari. Pinned down with ui describe-point: the End pill at AX (648,17) answers at touch (17,19), giving touch_x = ay, touch_y = L - ax where L is the long edge. Wired into tap_scroll; the Game Setup sheet opens on the first try now. Portrait needs no transform, which is why none of this showed up until tonight.
fixed: drivers/ios_tap.py to_touch() applies the transform whenever the app is landscape.
Setup, End and the score buttons all now hit their targets in landscape on the SE.
screen_size: third attempt, and the one that is rightiOS harnessINFOTwo earlier versions each broke something. Largest-single-frame picked a ScrollView content frame and reported a landscape SE as 375x667. Extent-of-all-frames blew up on scrolling content - the Home screen measured 2910x1340, which corrupted the tap transform and made every Home tap silently miss while in-game taps worked. It now reads the Application element, which IS the window: 667x375. With that fixed the whole landscape flow works - Record opens the picker, a sport starts, Setup opens, End Game completes.
Android rotation: settings writes do not stick, adb emu rotate doesAndroid harnessINFOsettings put system user_rotation 1 reads back as 0 on these emulators and the app stays on its "Rotate to landscape" placeholder - which cost time earlier tonight and made the app look stuck. adb -s <serial> emu rotate goes through the emulator console and works first time. Use that.
An ENDING GAME modal was eating every tap on Android HomeAndroid harnessINFOHome looked completely inert again - Record, the hero card, everything. No ANR this time. A screenshot showed an ENDING GAME / Saving game summary / Done dialog sitting over Home, and it does not surface in the text dump I was reading. Tapping Done freed it immediately. Second time tonight that "the screen is dead" turned out to be a modal I had not looked for; the first was iOS StoreKit. The rule is the same on both platforms: screenshot before concluding anything about unresponsive UI.
Direct coordinate taps skip the scroll-into-view guardiOS harnessINFOTwo failures this round came from the same mistake: computing a touch point straight from an element frame instead of going through tap_scroll. The scoring-mode control sits at y=442 on a 375pt-tall landscape screen - off-screen inside a scrollable sheet - so the tap landed on nothing and looked like the app ignoring it. Same for the volleyball picker row. If an element is reported below the screen height, it must be scrolled into view first; the tree happily reports things that are not rendered.
Android landscape text entry goes fullscreen and hides the whole screenAndroid harnessINFOTyping into a field in landscape put the keyboard into fullscreen extract mode - a black screen with just the text and a DONE button. Every dump after that showed only the app chrome, which read like the editor had failed to open. Tapping DONE restored it. Third variant tonight of "the screen looks broken but is actually covered": iOS StoreKit, the Android ENDING GAME dialog, and now the fullscreen keyboard.
Stale coordinates: pills widen as they gain counts, moving their neighboursAndroid harnessINFOTapped Foul six times with no effect and nearly filed it as a broken button. The stat pills grow when they gain a count (Foul becomes Foul 1), which pushes every pill to its right. The Foul centre had moved from x=2466 to x=2637, so my taps were landing on Blk - which is why Blk read 3. Re-dump immediately before every tap in a row of self-sizing controls; never reuse coordinates across a state change.
Fourth invisible modal: Stop Recording confirmationAndroid harnessINFOTapped STOP and the recording kept running; MediaStore stayed empty and it read like the save had failed. There was an Are you sure you want to stop recording? dialog waiting. Fourth variant tonight after iOS StoreKit, the Android ENDING GAME dialog, and the fullscreen keyboard.
ENDING GAME dialog is invisible to the accessibility treeAndroid harnessINFOAfter ending a game, three taps on Record did nothing and uiautomator dumps showed only the Home screen, so it read like Record was broken. A screenshot showed an ENDING GAME dialog (Saving game summary / Upload to YouTube / Done) sitting on top and eating every tap - it does not appear in the uiautomator dump at all. Two consequences: always screenshot rather than trusting the dump, and this dialog is likely invisible to screen readers too.
iPhone 17 Pro Max cannot be rotated - both rotation paths silently failiOS iPhone 17 Pro MaxSKIPThe Pro Max sim is stuck in portrait (440x956) and shows the app's own Rotate to landscape prompt. AppleScript through Simulator's Device > Rotate Left reports success but nothing moves, and Simulator's AX window list comes back EMPTY (window count 0) so the menu has no window to act on. idb ui rotate LANDSCAPE_LEFT also logs 'rotate succeeded' - it sends an HID event the simulator ignores. The SE is unaffected and still landscape (667x375), so nothing was broken by trying. Pro Max landscape fit is blocked until there is a working rotation path.
Pro Max rotation: third route (Simulator prefs plist) also failsiOS iPhone 17 Pro MaxSKIPSimulator stores per-device orientation at DevicePreferences.<UDID>.SimulatorWindowOrientation - the SE reads LandscapeRight/270, which is why it works. Writing LandscapeRight for the Pro Max does not stick: Simulator writes its LIVE state back over the pref, so the value reverts to Portrait. Writing it with Simulator quit would work, but Simulator refuses to quit while devices are booted (osascript quit returns and pgrep still finds it), and shutting the device down first still leaves the app holding state. So all three routes fail: AppleScript menu (AX window list is empty, count 0), idb ui rotate (logs success, sends an HID event the sim ignores), and the prefs plist (overwritten). Eric rotated the SE by hand once; that is the practical unblock for the Pro Max too.
Direct coordinate taps still skip the scroll guard - cost two failed tapsiOS harnessINFOTapped the iOS period picker by computing its centre from the accessibility tree and got no response twice; the readout never changed. The control was below the fold, so the computed coordinates landed on whatever was actually on screen. tap_scroll handled it ('Halves off-screen at y=389 - scrolled') and the tap took immediately. This is README trap 4/16 resurfacing: the tree reports elements that are not currently rendered, so ALWAYS go through tap_scroll rather than to_touch on a raw frame.
Kotlin: a second companion object silently shadows the firstAndroidINFOAdding for two constants unresolved TAG and every EXTRA_* across MainActivity - a class gets ONE companion, and the new one shadowed the existing one holding all of them. Five errors that looked unrelated to the change. Plain private properties instead. Worth remembering because the error messages point at the SHADOWED symbols, not at the companion you just added.
Do not invent handler names - read what the existing button callsAndroidINFOWiring the three score blocks I guessed at tennisPlayerWinsPoint / tennisUndoLastPoint / pickleballServingTeamScores / isTeam1Serving. All four were unresolved. The real names were advanceTennisPoint(isPlayer1:), the tennisUndoSnapshot restore pattern, advancePickleballPoint, and servingTeam which is an Int (1/2) not a Boolean. Eight compile errors from four guesses. Read the handler the existing control already invokes and reuse it verbatim - that is also what keeps the change behaviour-neutral.
tap_text matched a NON-CLICKABLE label and silently did nothingAndroidINFOCost two cycles and nearly a false 'disconnect is broken' conclusion. The confirm dialog has the word 'Disconnect' twice: a non-clickable label at (720,1430) and the actual button at (1161,1736). startsport_android.tap_text returns the FIRST text match and ignores the clickable flag it already parses, so it tapped the label, nothing happened, and the dialog stayed up looking like a failed teardown. Fixed: tap_text now prefers a clickable match and only falls back to a non-clickable one if there is no clickable candidate.
dump: [('Disconnect', 720, 1430, clickable=False), ('Disconnect', 1161, 1736, clickable=True)]
verify_recording searched the wrong directory and reported a STALE file as successiOSINFOCaught immediately, but it is exactly the kind of thing that manufactures a false PASS. The verifier looked only in `Documents/recordings`. Once Photos access is granted the app SAVES THE VIDEO TO THE PHOTOS LIBRARY and removes its own copy — the screen says 'Saved to Photos' — so the container is left holding only the recordings that FAILED and never got moved. My first run therefore returned an eight-hour-old volleyball file, printed a healthy duration and track list, and looked like a pass for a basketball test. Fixed: newest_recording() now searches the app container AND the simulator's Photos store at Devices/<udid>/data/Media/DCIM (recursively, skipping Thumbnails), taking the newest across both. GENERAL SHAPE: 'newest file in the place I expect' is not the same as 'the artefact this test just produced'. Check the identity of what you measured, not just its health — the stale file was perfectly valid, just irrelevant.
Container held only 0-byte pre-permission files; the real recording was at Devices/<udid>/data/Media/DCIM/100APPLE/IMG_0007.MP4

CORRECTION: the SE was in landscape the whole timeiOS harnessINFOI told Eric three times the simulator would not rotate. It had. A screenshot of the running app shows the scoring UI rendered sideways inside a portrait framebuffer: simctl io screenshot returns the device-native buffer without applying the rotation transform, and the accessibility tree reports frames in that same unrotated space. So both of my detection methods - screenshot aspect ratio and largest AX frame - report portrait for a landscape app. Neither is a valid orientation check. The right check is to look at the rendered content. This also means my earlier below-the-fold numbers were computed against the wrong axis: in landscape the usable height is the SHORTER dimension, so the y coordinates I was measuring run along the long edge. The measurements need redoing before the fit work can be trusted.

CORRECTION: iOS already had a BETTER auto-resize than the one I shipped on AndroidbothBUGI filed #48 to port Android auto-resize to iOS. Wrong direction. iOS already has FlexScoreMetrics (ScoreFrameUI.swift:1651, dated 2026-08-21) feeding the shared TeamScoreButton, and it is strictly better than what I built: it is landscape-only (same rule), it measures the ACTUAL available height against the rest of the panel content and shrinks ONLY when the panel genuinely cannot fit, it has a floor (76pt) so the target stays comfortable, and it scales score/name/icon fonts and padding off the resulting row height. My Android version is a device-height LADDER — it shrinks on a short screen even when that sport panel has room to spare. The iOS comment even records that exact mistake being made and fixed there first: an earlier flat-30% share "shrank the buttons on a volleyball panel that had a third of its height sitting empty — smaller targets for nothing". So the parity gap runs the other way, and Android should be upgraded to the measured approach rather than iOS being dragged down to a ladder.
My measured auto-resize was broken — reverted to the 1324 ladderAndroidBUGBuilt a measure-then-shrink version to match iOS FlexScoreMetrics. It misbehaved on first run: the log shows it shrinking three times on a basketball panel that ALREADY fitted (43 controls, 0 below the fold), with the reported overflow stuck at a constant 51px that never moved as the score shrank. So it walked straight to the smallest size and made targets smaller for nothing — the exact failure iOS's own comment warns about. Root cause not yet found; likely the score row height does not actually follow the text size, so shrinking the font cannot reduce content height. DISABLED and reverted to the 1324 device-height ladder, which is cruder but demonstrably helped and cannot loop.

Emulator-to-emulator pairing blocked: each AVD is its own NATAndroid emulator pairSKIPTried to cross-pair the two Android emulators. The camera advertises Wi-Fi 10.0.2.16 - the AVD private NAT address - which the other emulator cannot reach; Wi-Fi Direct (192.168.49.1) and Bluetooth are equally unreachable between AVDs. Bridged them with adb forward tcp:9876 on the camera plus adb reverse tcp:9876 on the controller, and the controller DID connect through it (RemoteScoringClient: "Reconnected to server on attempt #1 at 127.0.0.1") but was dropped instantly and repeatedly with "Server disconnected (EOF)", and the camera server logged no accept at all - the adb forward accepts the TCP handshake itself, so a successful connect() there proves nothing. Same-platform emulator pairing needs either real devices or a relay path.
BUG: a controller never inherited the camera phone Pro across platformsbothBUGEric: Android (free) controlling iOS (Pro) — the iOS camera showed players and stats, but the Android controller still had Advanced LOCKED in Game Setup. Root cause: the feature was built on Android only. Android sends recorderIsPro on STATE_UPDATE and consumes it into FeatureGate.sessionProOverride, clearing it on disconnect. iOS did NEITHER — it never sent the flag and never consumed one. So iOS-camera pairs (Eric's case) and iOS-controller pairs were both broken; only Android-to-Android worked. Fixed on both: iOS now sends recorderIsPro on CONNECTION_ACK and every STATE_UPDATE, consumes it into a new FeatureGate.sessionProOverride (precedence debugOverride ?? sessionProOverride ?? _realIsPro, mirroring Android), and clears it in teardownConnection. Android additionally now sends it on BOTH ACK builders (Bluetooth + TCP), not just STATE_UPDATE — the controller opens Game Setup immediately on connect, which is exactly where Eric saw the lock.
fixed: Android 1.1.1323 / iOS 703
Pro inheritance is tier-level, so it covers every sport by designbothPASSEric asked for it across all sports, not just volleyball. It needs no per-sport work: the borrowed tier lands on FeatureGate.isPro, which every Pro gate in both apps already reads — Advanced scoring, 4K, no watermark, unlimited highlights, custom colours, streaming, the lot. Scoped to the session and cleared on disconnect, so nothing on the controller is unlocked locally.
PROVEN ON THE WIRE: the iOS camera does send recorderIsProiOSPASSDecisive test instead of more reading. Forced the SE simulator to Pro via its own tier switcher key (xcrun simctl spawn <udid> defaults write com.scoreframe.ios debug_tier_override pro — same key Android uses), started a game, put it in Connect Nearby host mode, then connected the harness WireController as a synthetic controller and dumped what arrived. The CONNECTION_ACK contains recorderIsPro = true, alongside type/sport/state/protocolVersion 2 and platform ios. So the iOS SEND half works end to end on the real wire, and the ACK is well-formed for Android parseMessage. That eliminates the half I most suspected.
WireController ACK dump
Android consume path traced — reached, and only cleared on disconnectAndroidINFOThe ACK branch in RemoteScoringClient parses the raw line and calls parseAndUpdateGameState, which is where recorderIsPro is read into FeatureGate.sessionProOverride. The Advanced lock reads FeatureGate.isPro, which honours that override (not realIsPro). The only two clears are ConnectionManager.disconnect() and cleanUpClientConnection(), and the latter runs on dropped/reconnecting paths — correct behaviour, not a leak. So on paper the Android side should work, which means the remaining suspects are: the controller build predating the consume code (added 2026-08-20), a timing order where Game Setup is built before the ACK lands, or the controller not actually being in CLIENT role at the moment Game Setup runs.
Relay path was missing the flag on BOTH platforms — fixedbothBUGSeparate from Eric's local case but real: FirebaseRelayServer builds its OWN state payload on both Android and iOS and never included recorderIsPro, so an internet-relay scorer could never inherit Pro regardless of the local transports. Added to both servers, and the iOS relay CLIENT now adopts it and releases it on disconnect (Android has no relay client — the web page is the client there, and score.html has no Pro gating to inherit).
fixed: pending ship
Eric decisions recorded: symmetric downgrade, prompt on controllerbothPASSAnswered 2026-08-22. (1) His ANDROID controller is 1.1.1323+, so the consume code is definitely present and this is a real bug, not a stale build. (2) Camera Free + controller Pro should KEEP downgrading the controller — symmetric, prevents Pro output being obtained by borrowing a subscriber phone to keep score. Current behaviour is correct; no change. (3) Reconnect should PROMPT ON THE CONTROLLER ("<Camera> has a paused game — resume it?") rather than auto-resuming or asking the camera, since the camera is usually on a tripod. (4) IMPORTANT clarification: "I can see the players and stats on the camera phone but they are not on the controller phone" — that is the SAME borrowed-Pro bug, not a separate volleyball one. The controller is not Pro, so its Advanced stats section stays hidden. #43 and #49 are one bug.
Android controller DOES adopt the tier — proven by logAndroidPASSAdded a one-line SF-PRO log at the adoption site (trap 21: one log beats twenty minutes of reading). With a synthetic camera replaying a REAL captured iOS ACK, the controller logs "adopted recorderIsPro=true -> isPro=true" on every message, repeatedly, and NEVER clears while sitting on the screen. So both halves of borrowed-Pro are individually correct: iOS sends it (proven on the wire earlier) and Android consumes it.
REPRODUCED: leaving the discovery screen wipes the borrowed tierAndroidBUGNavigating away from the controller discovery screen logs "CLEARED sessionProOverride" with the stack: ConnectionManager.disconnect(ConnectionManager.kt:1102) <- RemoteDiscoveryActivity.onDestroy(RemoteDiscoveryActivity.kt:110). That is Eric's exact flow — connect as controller, leave that screen to start a game, find Advanced locked. The onDestroy IS guarded (if (!ConnectionManager.isConnected) { stopDiscovery(); disconnect() }), so the guard evaluated FALSE while the session was demonstrably alive: heartbeats were flowing to the camera and the tier was being adopted every 2s. Root cause is a STATE DESYNC — RemoteScoringClient had a live CONNECTED session while ConnectionManager.connectionState was not CONNECTED, because ConnectionManager.onConnected() (the only thing that sets it, :1031) was never called on this path. Same desync explains a second symptom seen throughout: the controller UI never left the discovery screen after connecting.
CAVEAT on the repro — synthetic camera may not drive onConnectedAndroidINFOBeing careful not to overclaim. The desync above was produced with fake_camera.py and a manual-IP connect. A REAL camera might call back through a path that does invoke ConnectionManager.onConnected(), in which case the guard would hold and Eric's failure would have a different trigger. What IS established regardless: (1) both halves of the tier handshake work, (2) there exists a reachable state where the client is connected and heartbeating while ConnectionManager believes it is not, and (3) in that state simply leaving the discovery screen silently tears down the session and the borrowed Pro. Next step is to confirm whether a real camera avoids it, then either fix onConnected not firing or make the teardown ask the CLIENT whether it is connected rather than the manager.
REAL iOS camera + REAL Android controller: borrowed Pro WORKSbothPASSThe pairing Eric reported failing. Emulator reaches the Mac LAN IP, and the iOS simulator binds there, so this is a genuine cross-platform pair rather than a synthetic one. Result: "Connecting to 192.168.86.40:9876" -> "Connected to server" -> "Connection acknowledged: sport=VOLLEYBALL" -> SF-PRO "adopted recorderIsPro=true -> isPro=true". So on this pair the tier IS inherited. Combined with the earlier wire capture (iOS sends it) and the adoption log (Android takes it), all three links of borrowed-Pro are now individually proven.
Borrowed Pro adopted on every run that actually connectedAndroidPASS3 of 3 real connects to the iOS camera logged "adopted recorderIsPro=true -> isPro=true". A 4th run showed no adoption and I nearly filed it as a reproduction of Eric's symptom — checked first: its client log is completely empty and the phone was still on Home, so the AUTOMATION never reached the Connect button and no connection was attempted. Harness flake, not a product failure. Worth recording because the tempting read ("1 in 4 connects loses Pro") would have sent the next session chasing nothing.
Controller ignored a PAUSED camera game — fixedAndroidBUGEric: connected fine but "the Android controller doesn't say anything about a game being able to resume". The controller only ever tested ServerGameState.ACTIVE (HomeActivity:1078 auto-launch and :1626 hero card); PAUSED was handled nowhere on the controller side — and PAUSED is exactly what a dropped-and-reconnected pair looks like. The hero card now treats PAUSED as resumable and offers "Resume <Sport> Game" instead of nothing. CAVEAT worth flagging: iOS DEFINES ServerGameState.PAUSED but nothing on iOS ever SETS it (only .ACTIVE/.ENDED/.IDLE are assigned), so an iOS camera will not yet report a paused game — the Android fix cannot fire for an iOS camera until that is added.
fixed: v1.1.1326
Borrowed Pro: full chain re-verified against a REAL iOS camerabothPASSInstrumented the exact transition Eric described. With the real iOS camera: "adopted recorderIsPro=true -> isPro=true", then "ConnectionManager.onConnected(192.168.86.40/Wi-Fi) -> CONNECTED", then on leaving the discovery screen "discovery onDestroy: manager.isConnected=true role=CLIENT clientAlive=true" — the teardown guard HOLDS and the tier survives. So the earlier desync (client connected while the manager was not) does NOT occur on the real path, and my leading explanation for Eric's failure is now also ruled out. His screenshot shows the controller genuinely running Free — padlock AND an ad banner, which only free tiers see — while connected. Cannot reproduce in the lab, so shipped an in-app diagnostic instead: the locked Advanced row now states whether the camera reported Free or whether no plan arrived at all.

Camera advertises ACTIVE + Pro — so BOTH of Eric's symptoms share one causebothPASSProbed the live iOS camera with wirectl in exactly Eric's configuration: CONNECTION_ACK says gameState=ACTIVE, sport=VOLLEYBALL, recorderIsPro=true. That reframes the whole thing. Off that single ACK the controller should do TWO things: adopt Pro, and offer/auto-launch the active game (HomeActivity:1078 auto-launches on ACTIVE, :1626 shows Join). Eric saw NEITHER — no resume offer and Advanced locked with an ad banner. So this is not two bugs (a Pro bug and a resume bug); it is one: on his hardware the controller is not acting on the camera ACK at all. Everything downstream of that ACK works in the lab.
Transport-difference hypothesis ruled outAndroidINFONext theory was that Eric paired over a transport whose reader does not parse the ACK — my lab tests all used Wi-Fi/TCP via manual IP. Checked: RemoteScoringClient has two reader threads, one for connectWithStreams (Bluetooth/stream) at :215 and one for the TCP socket at :338, and BOTH call parseAndUpdateGameState for CONNECTION_ACK and STATE_UPDATE (:231/:252 and :372/:404/:426). So the ACK is parsed on either transport and this does not explain it.
iOS PAUSED transition — deprioritised, with reasoniOSINFOI had flagged that iOS defines ServerGameState.PAUSED but never sets it, so the Android Resume-on-PAUSED fix could not fire for an iOS camera. Still true and still worth adding, but measurement dropped its priority: the iOS camera reports ACTIVE for a live game, and ACTIVE already has a controller path (auto-launch + Join card). So the missing PAUSED only matters for a camera that has parked a game and gone Home — a real recoverability case, but not the one Eric hit.
iOS PAUSED transition written but NOT VERIFIEDiOSFAILClosed the gap I had flagged: ServerGameState.PAUSED existed on iOS since the enum was written (its comment even says "recording paused or back to home") but nothing ever ASSIGNED it, so an iOS camera could never tell a controller it held a parked game and the Android Resume affordance had nothing to fire on. Added markGameParked()/markGameResumed() on CameraServerCoordinator, driven from the app scenePhase change. It compiles, and the camera correctly reports ACTIVE beforehand (verified by wire probe). BUT I could not make the transition FIRE: after backgrounding and re-foregrounding the sim, the log shows neither "game parked" nor "game resumed". Two things went wrong on the way and are worth keeping: (1) my first attempt logged with print(), which never reaches the unified log, so the check was silently blind — switched to os_log; (2) a backgrounded iOS app stops accepting connections, so the PAUSED state cannot be probed over the wire while it is in effect, which makes background the WRONG trigger for a state a controller is supposed to read. The useful trigger is probably the camera leaving the scoring screen while STAYING foregrounded ("back to home"), since only then is the server still there to be asked. Not shipped to Eric's phone — an unverified behaviour should not ride in release notes.
ROOT CAUSE: borrowed Pro — camera phone was running code older than the featureiOSPASSEric retested on 1.1.1326 and the diagnostic row I shipped for exactly this read "Advanced - no plan received from camera", i.e. sessionProOverride was NULL, not false: no message ever carried recorderIsPro. Traced it out: the Android client adopts the tier in parseAndUpdateGameState, which runs on BOTH the CONNECTION_ACK path and every STATE_UPDATE (~2s), so a timing race would self-heal - null means the field was absent from the wire entirely. iOS source on disk is complete (one ACK builder, one STATE_UPDATE builder, both carry the field). The answer was in the dates: RemoteScoringServer.swift gained recorderIsPro at 2026-08-22 11:20, and the NEWEST iOS build ever installed on any of Eric physical devices was 2026-08-19 20:30 - three days earlier. His camera phone was running code that predates the feature. That is why every lab test passed: simulators get a fresh build every cycle, his phones do not. Not a race, not a product bug. Fixed by building 705 (Release, per the direct-install rule) and installing on the paired SE. LESSON: when a feature works in the lab and fails in the field, date the binary on the field device before theorising about the code.

Next attempt should reconcile the two coordinate spaces first (or drive the sim in portrait) rather than re-deriving the game flow.
BUG I INTRODUCED: the park trigger would PAUSE a live gameiOSBUGFound while verifying, and it is mine. The trigger I wrote last cycle read: if newMode == .cameraServer resume else park. But .cameraServer is only the dedicated camera-server screen - a camera hosting from an ordinary game sits in .standaloneWithConfig (ScoreFrameApp.swift:847/857/1553). So ENTERING a normal hosted game takes the else branch and parks a game the user is actively scoring, telling any controller the game is PAUSED. It did not show up in the wire probe (still ACTIVE) purely because the mode changes before the server is attached, so markGameParked guard on server != nil swallowed it - ordering luck, not correctness, and it would bite as soon as that ordering shifted. iOS camera DOES send recorderIsPro on the wire (independent confirmation)iOSPASSIncidental but useful corroboration of the #49 root cause: a live iOS camera on a current build answered the handshake with recorderIsPro=True. SETTLED: iOS PAUSED is unreachable — trigger removed rather than left to misfireiOSPASSAnswers #52 and closes the iOS half of #50. iOS has NO foregrounded exit from a live game: the scoring screen carries no back/Home control, the brand bar is inert, and every mode = .home site either tears the session down (cancelling initial scheduled setup, ContentView:900) or follows a delete (GamePageView:1103). In .cameraServer mode even ENDING a game deliberately stays in the mode (ScoreFrameApp:6288 keeps the listener alive so the controller need not re-pair). The only exits are End -> ENDED or backgrounding, and a backgrounded app stops accepting connections, so a state set there can never be read by the controller it exists for. So ServerGameState.PAUSED cannot be reached on an iOS camera and any trigger for it can only misfire - as the one I wrote did, parking live games. REMOVED the trigger and both coordinator methods, leaving the reasoning in the file so nobody re-adds them. This costs nothing: a held iOS game stays ACTIVE and the controller ACTIVE path already joins it (HomeActivity:1078/:1626). PAUSED remains correct and useful on ANDROID, where the camera really can leave the game screen to Home. Builds clean.
RETRACTED — #51 sport leak: the instance serving the wire is NOT the instance being updatediOSINFODiagnosed but deliberately NOT fixed at the tail of a cycle - it is a protocol path and deserves a focused pass. Evidence: (1) a live iOS camera on a VOLLEYBALL game ACKs sport=HOCKEY; (2) scoring a point does NOT repair it, which rules out the simple stale-cache theory, because sendStateSync/updateCurrentSport are supposed to refresh exactly that; (3) there are TWO construction sites - AppState.server (ScoreFrameApp:1167) and CameraManager s (ContentView:6300) - and only the ContentView one is given liveSportProvider, the guard specifically written for this failure. So updates land on one object while the wire is served by another: the same two-instances hazard as the Android dual ScoreboardRenderer. Most likely full story: the TCP server survives across games (started during an earlier hockey game, where start() set currentSport and Self.active), and the later volleyball game never refreshed THAT object. Impact is real - a controller builds its whole scoring panel from the ACK sport, so it would render a hockey panel for a volleyball game. NEXT STEP that disambiguates in one run: log the ObjectIdentifier of the instance answering CONNECTION_ACK and compare it with RemoteScoringServer.active and AppState.server. RETRACTED: the #51 sport leak was my measurement error, not a bugiOSPASSWithdrawing the bug I filed last cycle. It was never real. TWO simulators were booted (17 Pro Max and SE) and they SHARE the Mac localhost - whichever ScoreFrame binds 127.0.0.1:9876 first owns it. lsof showed PID 4831 holding the socket from the SE bundle path, while I had been driving the 17 Pro Max. Two real lessons kept as traps: identify the socket OWNER before attributing behaviour to a device, and remember that a Debug build hides its code in ScoreFrame.debug.dylib, which is why my binary probes of the main executable all came back empty.

isConnected now consults the client — happy path intact, but the RACE itself was not reproducedAndroidINFOBeing explicit about what this run does and does not prove, because the tempting read is 'fixed and verified' and it is not. CHANGE: ConnectionManager.isConnected is now `connectionState == CONNECTED || remoteScoringClient?.isConnected() == true`. The old form trusted a flag that only onConnected() sets, so a client that was genuinely connected and heartbeating could read as disconnected — and RemoteDiscoveryActivity.onDestroy then called disconnect() and wiped the borrowed tier (reproduced 08-22 13:13). WHAT THIS RUN SHOWS: connect to a Pro fake camera over manual IP, leave the discovery screen. Log reads 'discovery onDestroy: manager.isConnected=true role=CLIENT clientAlive=true', there is NO 'released borrowed tier' line, and adoption keeps firing every 2s afterwards ('adopted recorderIsPro=true -> isPro=true'). Home shows 'Connected to Camera Phone'. So the happy path is healthy and my change causes no regression. WHAT IT DOES NOT SHOW: 'ConnectionManager.onConnected(10.0.2.2/Wi-Fi) -> CONNECTED' fired at 23:52:19.261, a full 1.5s BEFORE onDestroy. connectionState was therefore already CONNECTED and the OLD code would have passed the guard too. My new clause was never the thing that saved it. The desync is a race — onConnected posts via runOnUiThread and can lose to onDestroy — and I did not reproduce that race here. LIKELY WHY IT NO LONGER REPRODUCES: on 08-22 fake_camera did not ACK until it received HELLO. I changed it tonight to ACK on accept (matching the real server), so the handshake now completes far sooner and onConnected wins the race comfortably. I may have removed the repro conditions while fixing the harness. STATUS: correct by construction, no regression, NOT proven against the actual failure mode.

FIXED (iOS 1.2.244/714) — ERIC ON 713: resume CTA appears but (a) camera never enters the game, (b) WRONG SPORT on reconnectiOSBUGTwo distinct defects on top of 713, both mine. The CTA itself works — my gameState=PAUSED fix did land — but the flow behind it does not. (a) CAMERA DOES NOT FOLLOW. I made announceExistingGameIfAny() deliberately send-only, reasoning it 'cannot disturb a game already on screen'. Android's equivalent also invokes onGameResumeReceived LOCALLY so the camera relaunches its own game screen. Without that the iPhone sits on 'connected to server' forever while the controller goes into the game. Eric's call: match Android, guarded so it only fires when the camera is NOT already in a game. (b) WRONG SPORT — root cause found. sendConnectionAck computes `let liveSport = liveSportProvider?() ?? currentSport` (RemoteScoringServer.swift:538), and liveSportProvider is wired to CameraManager.currentSport (ContentView.swift:6323), which DEFAULTS TO "BASKETBALL" (ContentView.swift:4848) whenever the phone is sitting on Home rather than in a game. My adoptPausedSessionStateIfAny() set the SERVER's currentSport — which liveSportProvider overrides. So the ACK advertised BASKETBALL while a VOLLEYBALL game was paused, and the controller offered to resume the wrong sport. I set the wrong variable, and the lab missed it because there the camera had been in a volleyball game moments earlier so CameraManager.currentSport happened to agree.
fixed: (a) ScoreFrameApp.onGameLifecycle gained a RESUME branch mirroring START — navigates the camera into .cameraServer and sets gameState=.ACTIVE, guarded on `gameStarted != true` so it cannot disturb a live game; RemoteScoringServer now invokes that delegate locally as well as sending. (b) pausedSessionSport is stamped alongside gameState=.PAUSED and OUTRANKS liveSportProvider in sendConnectionAck, so a paused game advertises its own sport instead of CameraManager's Home-screen BASKETBALL default.

TestPlatformDetail
Per-sport analysis for the FINAL scoreboard - baseball is NOT automaticDesignINFOEric asked to analyse each sport before building the final-score scoreboard, and said volleyball and baseball know automatically. Read the code: volleyball does (matchComplete), and so do TENNIS (TennisScorer.kt:107), PICKLEBALL and BOWLING (BowlingScorer.isGameComplete) - none of which he mentioned. BASEBALL DOES NOT: there is no end-of-game detection anywhere in the baseball code, and it is the worst candidate for inference - home team leading after the top of the 9th ends it with no bottom half, walk-offs end it mid-inning, ties go to extras, and youth games are often 6 or 7 innings or mercy-ruled. No scheduled length is stored, so any hard-coded rule is wrong for someone. Also surfaced two edge cases with no answer in the code today: DRAWS (soccer/hockey can end level - gold-highlighting a winner is meaningless at 2-2) and BOWLING (usually one player, so nobody to beat). Recommended one shared isFinal flag plus a derived winner enum so both platforms and every renderer read the same thing. Artifact: https://claude.ai/code/artifact/3a0f4428-4d41-4581-b305-f0b6990531e8
FINAL scoreboard state built and verified (v1.1.1316)Android S26 Ultra (emulator-5554)PASSFirst increment of the mode x final work Eric approved. Domain logic follows the per-sport analysis: volleyball/tennis/pickleball read matchComplete, bowling reads BowlingScorer.isGameComplete, everything else uses a manual Mark Final toggle - baseball deliberately manual because walk-offs, extras and 6/7-inning youth games make any hard-coded rule wrong for someone. gameWinner is NOT always the bigger number (volleyball/tennis/pickleball decide on sets/games), draws return DRAW rather than an invented winner, and solo bowling returns NONE. Rendering state lives on ScoreboardRenderer rather than being added to all twelve ScoreboardType classes, which would touch the remote protocol and persistence for each; a uniform wrapWithFinalBanner covers every sport from the same seam the event-label banner uses. Verified on basketball: 0-0 renders FINAL - DRAW in white, 1-0 renders FINAL in gold, and the Mark Final tile toggles to a filled gold FINAL. Correctly ABSENT on tennis, which auto-detects.

#30 CONFIRMED with evidence: long football names OVERLAP (not clip) — burned into videoAndroidBUGEric asked me to extend dynamic name width to the remaining scoreboards. Checked the premise first and it was WRONG in the usual direction - they ALL already grow with the name, just with bespoke arithmetic instead of the shared dynamicNameAreaWidth() helper: Volleyball, Soccer simple + advanced, Football and Baseball each measure the widest name and widen the board. So there was nothing to add. But two of them CLAMP that growth: Football coerceIn(0f, 260f) per side, Baseball the same. That is the actual defect, and I reproduced it on the emulator rather than reasoning about it. At 32-34 character names ("Westside Consolidated Thunderhawks") the board grows and both names render perfectly. At 51-56 characters ("Northern Valley Fighting Badgers Junior Varsity Team") the board stops growing at the clamp while the text keeps drawing full length, so THE TWO TEAM NAMES DRAW STRAIGHT THROUGH EACH OTHER - an unreadable jumble across the middle of the board, over the score. It is overlap, not truncation, which is the worse failure: truncation still reads. Screenshot saved as evidence_football_overlap.png. Threshold sits between 34 (fine) and 51 (broken); JV/Varsity suffixes on school team names reach that length plausibly. This one is burned into recorded video, so it is not cosmetic.
tools/e2e/evidence_football_overlap.png
#30 FIXED + SHIPPED 1.1.1330: long names ellipsize instead of overlappingAndroidPASSFixed the overlap I reproduced, and verified both directions on the emulator rather than trusting the code. TWO PARTS, because raising the clamp alone would only move the cliff: (1) football and baseball now allow 520px of growth per side instead of 260, which covers ordinary school-team names carrying a Junior Varsity / Varsity Squad suffix; (2) a new fitToWidth() helper ellipsizes each name to the room its side actually has (base room + allowed growth - logo slot), so the names CANNOT overlap at any length, however absurd. The clamp exists for a real reason - an unbounded bitmap gets scaled down by the GL quad until the text is unreadable on video - so removing it outright would have traded one unreadable board for another. VERIFIED BOTH WAYS: at 51-56 characters the names now render as "Northern Valley Fighting Badgers Junior…" and "Westside Consolidated Regional Thunder…" with the score clean and legible between them, where before they drew through each other. And the regression case matters just as much - at 32-34 characters both names still render in FULL with no ellipsis, so nothing that used to fit got needlessly truncated. Also fixed the home logo slot to measure the FITTED text rather than the full string, otherwise the logo would have been positioned off the end of a name that is no longer drawn that long. Evidence saved: evidence_football_overlap.png (before) and evidence_football_fixed.png (after). Shipped 1.1.1330.
fixed: ScoreboardRenderer.kt — fitToWidth() + clamps 260->520
tools/e2e/evidence_football_fixed.png
#30 PARITY: iOS had the identical overlap — ported and installed as build 707iOSPASSThe parity rule says an Android change gets ported the same session, and this one needed it: iOS was NOT merely similar, the clamps are an exact port of Android line for line - min(max(0, fbLogoSlot + textWidth(awayName) - 280), 260) for football and min(max(0, bbLogoSlot + bbWidest - 200), 260) for baseball. Same 260 ceiling, same 280/301/200 base rooms, so iOS overlapped at exactly the same name lengths as the Android board I photographed. Worth noting iOS ALSO has its own dynamicNameAreaWidth() and that one is unclamped, so the other sports were never at risk - the defect was confined to the two boards that clamp, on both platforms. Ported the same two-part fix: clamps 260 -> 520, and a Swift fitToWidth() that ellipsizes each name to the room its side actually has. Also measured the FITTED string for the home logo slot, matching the Android fix, or the logo would sit off the end of a name no longer drawn that long. VERIFIED BY A BETTER TOOL THAN I EXPECTED: ScoreboardGrowthTests exists precisely to assert that iOS and Android size these boards to identical numbers - its own comment says the two platforms must match or the same game looks different depending on which phone filmed it. All 4 pass, so the raised clamp did not break sizing parity and short names still produce exactly the old dimensions. Then the SE accepted an install for the first time tonight, so build 707 is now ON THE PHONE carrying both this and the Event Name gate fix.
fixed: ScoreboardTextureCache.swift — fitToWidth() + 3 clamps 260->520

Home 4 pass · 4 bug · 5 info · 2 skip

TestPlatformDetail
CRASH: HomeActivity NPE on relaunch (setDecorPadding)AndroidBUGFATAL EXCEPTION twice tonight (03:28:10 and 04:14:50) — java.lang.NullPointerException: Attempt to invoke virtual method void androidx.appcompat.widget.ContentFrameLayout.setDecorPadding(int,int,int,int) on a null object reference, at AppCompatDelegateImpl.applyFixedSizeWindow -> ensureSubDecor -> setContentView, from HomeActivity.buildLayout(HomeActivity.kt:1390). CONFIRMED FACTS: it happens on handleRelaunchActivity (a configuration-change relaunch, i.e. rotation), it kills the app to the launcher, and buildLayout() calls setContentView(root) while being invoked from 15 separate places in HomeActivity. HYPOTHESIS, not verified: repeatedly calling setContentView on an AppCompatActivity lets a config-change relaunch rebuild the subdecor while a previous setContentView is in flight, leaving mContentView null. Surfaced by rotation-heavy automation, so real-world frequency is unknown — but it is a real crash on the launcher activity, not a harness artefact.
logcat 08-22 03:28 and 04:14
HomeActivity crash — mechanism identified by readingAndroidPASSUpgrading last cycle's guess to a code-verified chain. (1) HomeActivity.onCreate:1026 installs ConnectionManager.onStateChanged = { runOnUiThread { ... buildLayout() ... } } — a lambda capturing the Activity. (2) HomeActivity.onDestroy:657 does NOT clear it; it only calls RealtimeSyncManager.stopListening(). (3) ConnectionManager.onStateChanged is nulled ONLY in release(), which runs on app exit/sign-out. (4) ConnectionManager fires it via mainHandler.post at ConnectionManager.kt:1272, so the invocation can land AFTER the Activity is gone. On a config-change relaunch the old HomeActivity is destroyed with its lambda still installed on the singleton; a posted connection-state change then calls buildLayout() -> setContentView() on the dead instance, AppCompat s subdecor is gone, and ContentFrameLayout is null -> the NPE seen at 03:28 and 04:14. Secondary consequence: the singleton holds a destroyed Activity, so this is also a leak.
HomeActivity.kt:657,1026,1180,1390 + ConnectionManager.kt:152,1260,1272
Crash does NOT reproduce from rotation aloneAndroidINFOTwo targeted attempts, both negative: 20 straight rotations on Home (no crash), and 12 iterations of rotating 0.6s into HomeActivity startup to force a launch race (no crash). That is consistent with the mechanism above — the crash also needs a CONNECTION-STATE CHANGE to fire inside the destroy/recreate window, which my sweeps produced incidentally by starting and ending games. Reproducing it deliberately needs a second device to drive connection state, so it stays unreproduced-on-demand for now.
Recommended fix (NOT applied — flagged for Eric)AndroidSKIPTwo lines, both defensive: (a) clear the callback in HomeActivity.onDestroy — if (ConnectionManager.onStateChanged === thisCallback) ConnectionManager.onStateChanged = null, keeping a reference so a newer instance is not clobbered; (b) guard the body with if (isFinishing || isDestroyed) return@runOnUiThread. Deliberately NOT applied: I found this crash, Eric did not ask for it, it touches all of Home, and I said last cycle it was his call. The diagnosis is done so the fix is cheap whenever he wants it.
CORRECTION: my crash diagnosis was wrongAndroidBUGI reported the HomeActivity NPE as a stale ConnectionManager.onStateChanged callback firing buildLayout() on a DEAD Activity after rotation, and called that chain verified. It is not. The full stack reads "Unable to start activity ComponentInfo{...HomeActivity}" from performLaunchActivity — the crash happens while the Activity is being CREATED, not from a callback on a destroyed one. My isFinishing/isDestroyed guard in buildLayout never fired once (0 hits across 18 rotations) precisely because the activity is alive at that moment. What is actually failing is setContentView -> AppCompatDelegateImpl.ensureSubDecor -> applyFixedSizeWindow with a null ContentFrameLayout, during a config-change relaunch. Cause still unknown; it is intermittent (1 crash in ~28 rotations, 0/3 trials of 8 rotations each). I read the callback code, found a real leak, and wrongly assumed it explained the crash I happened to be looking at.
The callback cleanup is still valid — as a LEAK fixAndroidPASSKept, but re-scoped honestly. 12 singleton callbacks captured an Activity and were never cleared, so every rotation left a destroyed HomeActivity/MainActivity reachable and still being invoked. onDestroy now clears them; the shared onServerGameStateChanged is cleared by IDENTITY because MainActivity sets that same field and clears it itself at :10720, so a blind null would pull the rug from a live MainActivity. Release notes corrected to claim a leak fix only.
fixed: v1.1.1321
HomeActivity crash — NOT reproducible on demand (6 strategies, ~80 cycles)AndroidINFOTried to earn a reliable repro so a fix could be verified. All negative: (1) 20 straight rotations on Home; (2) 12 launch-races rotating 0.6s into startup; (3) 3 trials of 8 rotations; (4) 14 rotations with always_finish_activities=1 forcing constant recreation; (5) 10 nav+rotate cycles diving into a game page and back, which is what the app was doing when it crashed for real; (6) 4 post-install trials rotating during the slow cold-cache first launch, since all three real crashes followed a fresh process. Roughly 80 cycles, zero reproductions. The two overnight crashes (03:28, 04:14) and one at 07:46 all happened during UNATTENDED sweeps under heavier load than I can recreate deliberately. CONCLUSION: I cannot verify any fix for this, so I am not going to write one. Restored always_finish_activities to 0 afterwards.
Candidate mitigation, deliberately NOT appliedAndroidSKIPHomeActivity declares no android:configChanges, so every rotation fully recreates a screen whose buildLayout() calls setContentView and is invoked from 15 places. Adding configChanges=orientation|screenSize|uiMode would stop the recreation and therefore this crash path entirely. NOT applied: it is a real behaviour change (the activity would have to re-layout itself on rotation instead of being rebuilt), I have no repro to verify it against, and I have already been wrong about this crash twice. Flagged for Eric rather than shipped on a hunch.
The setDecorPadding crash finally has a CALLER — and a likely link to Eric's Pro symptomAndroidBUGThe crash I could not reproduce in ~80 rotation cycles fired during a controller connect landing on Home, with a full stack for the first time: HomeActivity.buildLayout(HomeActivity.kt:1432) <- HomeActivity.initHomeScreen(HomeActivity.kt:371). Line 1432 is setContentView(root) — the same statement as the old :1390, renumbered by my earlier edit. The NEW information is the caller: this is Home FIRST build during startup, not a stale-callback rebuild, which kills the last remnant of my original (already retracted) explanation. STRONG HYPOTHESIS, not yet proven: this crash is what Eric actually hit. The app dies on connect, Android restarts it, and the fresh process has no remote session — so no borrowed tier, and Advanced reads locked. That would explain a Pro symptom with correct Pro plumbing. Still intermittent: 0 crashes on 2 immediate repeats. Theme hypothesis RULED OUT — Theme.GameFrame/Theme.ScoreFrame are identical across values, values-night, values-v31 and values-night-v31 (all Material3.DayNight.NoActionBar, no fixed width/height attrs).
Re-entrancy hypothesis NOT supportedAndroidINFOBest remaining theory for the setDecorPadding NPE was a re-entrant buildLayout: it calls setContentView, is invoked from 15 places including callbacks that can fire mid-build, and AppCompat sub-decor is not re-entrancy safe. Added a guard that skips a nested build and LOGS when it fires, so the theory could be falsified rather than assumed. Across 4 controller-connect runs it never fired once, and the crash did not recur (1 crash in 7 connect attempts total). So re-entrancy is not the mechanism on this path. Keeping the guard as a cheap defensive measure — a nested rebuild is redundant anyway — but labelling it unproven rather than a fix.
Connected rendered RED on a red palette — fixedAndroidBUGEric: "on the controller home screen the connection status says connected but that area is a red color ... it should always be a bright green connected color", and separately the hero card when connected. Cause: the banner used ScoreFrameUI.live(), which resolves to sf_live = the THEME PRIMARY, so on a red palette connected renders red. The hero card used success(), which is no safer — palettes override sfSuccess too (Trailhead maps it to its primary, Floodlight to its live colour). Every themable route could paint connected as a warning. Added ScoreFrameUI.connected(): deliberately NOT themable, two shades only for light/dark contrast, both unmistakably green. Green-means-connected is a convention people arrive with.
fixed: v1.1.1326
Home rotation crash: does NOT reproduce on either build — closing the huntAndroidINFOWent after the one NEXT item not blocked on Eric. First finding: task #45 (the configChanges mitigation) was a DECISION NOT TO SHIP - HomeActivity carries no configChanges attribute in the manifest, so Home still fully recreates on rotation and the crash window is still open. Then tried to force it. 24 rotations on 1.1.1325 across two profiles: a settled Home, and the harsher rotate-during-startup variant (relaunch, then rotate at 1s intervals while sections are still populating, x4 rounds) which is the shape most likely to land a callback on a dead Activity. No ScoreFrame crash - the only NullPointerException in logcat is Google TapAndPay/NFC, unrelated. Ran the SAME protocol against emulator-5556 on 1.1.1313, which predates the callback-clearing work, as a control: also no crash. So the differential is inconclusive and my protocol is what is wrong, not the builds. I also checked the obvious missing precondition - 5554 is SIGNED IN with cloud data and a parked Basketball game, so the RealtimeSync/ConnectionManager listeners that my (already retracted) mechanism theory depended on were live, and it still did not crash. CONCLUSION: seen twice in earlier overnight runs, never reproducible on demand, still no proven mechanism, and I am not going to invent a third one. The defensive work already shipped (buildLayout re-entrancy wrapper, isFinishing/isDestroyed guard, onDestroy clearing 9 callbacks) is the right posture for a rare unreproducible race. Recommend leaving it closed unless it recurs WITH a stack trace, which is the only thing that would actually move this forward. Eric had already said leave it.
RETRACTED — HOME CRASH REPRODUCED WITH A STACK TRACE — reopening; trigger is CONNECT, not rotationAndroidINFOLast cycle I closed this as unreproducible and wrote "leave closed unless it recurs WITH a stack trace". It recurred one cycle later, with one, while I was doing something else entirely. The trigger is NOT rotation - which is exactly why 24 rotations across two builds never produced it. It fired one second after the controller connected to the camera (connect 19:32:02, crash 19:32:03). Full chain with our lines: HomeActivity.onCreate(HomeActivity.kt:363) -> initHomeScreen(:371) -> buildLayout(:1233) -> buildLayoutInner(:1460) -> setContentView() -> AppCompatDelegateImpl.setContentView:763 -> NullPointerException calling ContentFrameLayout.setDecorPadding on null. So it dies inside performLaunchActivity during onCreate, on the FIRST setContentView, with AppCompat sub-decor not in the state setContentView expects. Not deterministic: three plain relaunches afterwards were clean (the connection was gone by then), so it needs the connect path specifically - consistent with a race seen only twice in months. NOTE this also vindicates the shape of my original (retracted) theory - a connection-driven path reaching buildLayout during Activity creation - though the retraction was still right at the time because I had no evidence. NEXT: reproduce by connecting as controller repeatedly, and look at what launches HomeActivity on the connect path and whether it can re-enter buildLayout before the decor exists. —— RETRACTED: Superseded by the fix in 1.1.1327 - root cause found and verified, see the FIXED entry.
FIXED + SHIPPED 1.1.1327: connect-path Home crash was CLEAR_TOP without SINGLE_TOPAndroidPASSRoot-caused, fixed and verified in one pass. Repro rate first: looping the controller connect gave 1 crash in 4 - a real rate to measure against, versus twice in months. The launch log named the culprit: at the moment of the crash the app relaunched HomeActivity with flg=0x4000000, which is FLAG_ACTIVITY_CLEAR_TOP with NO FLAG_ACTIVITY_SINGLE_TOP. HomeActivity declares no launchMode, so it is standard - and by documented Android semantics CLEAR_TOP alone FINISHES the existing instance and creates a new one. That destroy+recreate is what races AppCompat: the new instance reaches setContentView (HomeActivity.kt:1460 via onCreate:363 -> initHomeScreen:371 -> buildLayout:1233) while the sub-decor is not in the state it expects, and dies on ContentFrameLayout.setDecorPadding of null. The two offending launches are RemoteDiscoveryActivity.kt:185 and :1114 - both on the CONNECT path, which is exactly why 24 rotations never found it. HomeChrome.kt already pairs the flags correctly at four sites, so the fix had a precedent in the same codebase: pair CLEAR_TOP with SINGLE_TOP so the instance is REUSED via onNewIntent, which HomeActivity already implements (:571). Neither site passes extras, so nothing is lost by not re-running onCreate. VERIFIED: 0 crashes in 12 connects after the fix, against 1-in-4 before - at that baseline a clean 12 would happen by chance about 3% of the time, and the mechanism is deterministic rather than statistical. Tier adoption stayed green throughout (12/12). Shipped as 1.1.1327 to App Distribution with notes written in the same edit as the version bump.
fixed: RemoteDiscoveryActivity.kt:185,1114 — CLEAR_TOP or SINGLE_TOP
#54 audit: 9 more CLEAR_TOP-only launches paired, shipped 1.1.1328AndroidPASSSwept every FLAG_ACTIVITY_CLEAR_TOP in the app (36 sites) for the shape that caused the 1327 crash. Found NINE more unpaired launches targeting HomeActivity - MainActivity.kt:10571, 10725, 10780, 11028, 11104, 31641, 40019, 40948 and SettingsActivity.kt:236 - all return-to-Home paths (ending a game, backing out, leaving Settings), each able to finish and recreate Home exactly like the connect path did. Paired all nine with FLAG_ACTIVITY_SINGLE_TOP. Two sites were deliberately NOT changed after checking them: HomeActivity.kt:9759 targets GameHistoryActivity and PASSES EXTRAS (show_team_id/show_team_name), and GameHistoryActivity has no onNewIntent at all - pairing there would silently drop those extras whenever it was already on top, trading a rare crash for a routine wrong-screen bug. The notification launches in ScoreFrameMessagingService use NEW_TASK|CLEAR_TOP, a different pattern, and were left alone. Verified the behaviour change rather than assuming it: reusing Home instead of recreating it could have shown stale content, so I ran a full start-game -> End Game -> Home flow and confirmed Home renders completely AND the just-ended game appears in RECENT GAMES (onResume refresh covers what the recreate used to). Zero crashes through the flow. Shipped 1.1.1328.
fixed: MainActivity.kt x8 + SettingsActivity.kt:236 — CLEAR_TOP or SINGLE_TOP

Architecture 1 bug · 1 info

TestPlatformDetail
Systemic: 10 singleton callbacks capture an Activity, none are clearedAndroidBUGThe HomeActivity crash is one instance of a pattern, not a one-off. Assigned from Activities and never nulled on destroy: HomeActivity installs 7 on ConnectionManager (onGameStartReceived:931, onGameEndReceived:954, onGameResumeReceived:987, onGameResetReceived:1000, onNewGameReceived:1005, onServerGameStateChanged:1018, onStateChanged:1026) plus RealtimeSyncManager.onDataChanged:11790; MainActivity installs 3 more (onServerGameStateChanged:10684, onServerStreamStateChanged:31799, onServerStreamPillRefresh:31811). Neither onDestroy clears any of them, and ConnectionManager only nulls them in release() (app exit / sign-out). RealtimeSyncManager.stopListening() IS called from HomeActivity.onDestroy but it only removes Firestore listeners — it does not clear onDataChanged. All of these callbacks touch UI (refreshResumeCard / refreshQuickStats / refreshSections / updateConnectionStatus / buildLayout), so after a config-change relaunch they run against a destroyed Activity and keep it alive. Only onStateChanged fails loudly, because it is the one that reaches setContentView; the other nine leak and operate on detached views quietly. NOT FIXED — reported for Eric.
HomeActivity.kt / MainActivity.kt / ConnectionManager.kt / RealtimeSyncManager.kt
Correction: it is 12 uncleaned callbacks, not 10AndroidINFOCounted exactly rather than from memory: HomeActivity assigns 7 ConnectionManager callbacks AND 2 RealtimeSyncManager ones (onDataChanged + onFollowRequest); MainActivity assigns 3. That is 12 total, none cleared in onDestroy. My earlier report said 10 because I credited RealtimeSyncManager with one callback instead of two.

FIXED + SHIPPED 1.1.1329: tickets could not be sent from inside a gameAndroidPASSEric hit "Failed: Object cannot be encoded in JSON: VOLLEYBALL" trying to report the roster crash. GameSession.sport is a ScoreboardMode ENUM and FeedbackActivity.kt:319 put it straight into the Firestore document; Firestore cannot encode a Kotlin enum, so the whole ticket write failed. Fixed to .name. The nasty part is WHEN it fires: gameSession is only non-null during an ACTIVE game, so the failure was exclusive to in-game reports - which is precisely when someone is reporting an in-game bug. The reports most worth having were the only ones that could never arrive, and it would have looked like silence rather than breakage. Bigger consequence: the ticket payload also carries lastCrashStack, recentLogs and a 3000-line logcatDump from DiagnosticLog, which installs an uncaught-exception handler and persists the stack to a file across launches. So this one bug was also blocking the delivery mechanism for every crash report. Audited the class before shipping - every other "sport" field headed for Firestore is already a String (ScoringEvent, TimelineEvent) or goes through toProtocolString() (FirebaseRelayServer); this was the only enum instance. Shipped 1.1.1329.
fixed: FeedbackActivity.kt:326 — gameSession?.sport?.name

#55 ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED 1.1.1331: roster-back crash was a re-parented button rowAndroidPASSEric ticket SF-CUF58H delivered the stack (the 1329 encoder fix worked - currentSport came through as the string VOLLEYBALL). Cause: IllegalStateException "The specified child already has a parent" at MainActivity.applyPortraitButtonSizing:2448, reached from applyOrientationLayout via the sportSettingsLauncher result callback. The method detached only TWO of six buttons before re-adding them. First pass that is fine - the six are direct children of buttonRow, so removeAllViews() genuinely orphans them. Someone had hit this before with qsBtn and bufferBtn and patched just those two. Now all six are detached. WHY MY SWEEP MISSED IT, which is the useful part: applyPortraitButtonSizing is PORTRAIT-ONLY and startsport_android.py forces landscape on every run, so three lab reproductions in a row could never touch the code path. FIXED 1.1.1332: "Camera unavailable" fired over a working previewAndroidPASSReported with a screenshot showing the dialog on top of a live camera image, and Eric then pinned the trigger himself - it happens coming back to scoring FROM THE ROSTER, the same path as the 1331 crash. That closed it: the sportSettingsLauncher callback calls applyOrientationLayout(force = true), the rebuild tears down and recreates the camera surface, and CameraEngine.isRendering is nothing but renderThread != null - a reference that surfaceDestroyed also nulls. So it reads false during ANY teardown, not only a real failure. The existing guard waited 2500ms and then sampled ONCE; if that single look landed in the rebuild gap it announced a failure over a perfectly good preview. Fixed by requiring RENDER_RECOVERY_STRIKES=4 consecutive misses at 1200ms apart after the same 2500ms grace, so a transient null cannot trigger it while a camera that genuinely never returns is still reported within about 7 seconds. Note the honest trade: a real failure now takes ~7s to announce instead of 2.5s, which is the right side to err on given the dialog tells people recording is off. Shipped 1.1.1332.
fixed: MainActivity.kt — consecutive-strike recovery probe

Something in that chain is not what I think it is. Also important, from Eric: the message is NEW, he has never seen it before - so this is a regression, not a long-standing quirk. It now reproduces WITHOUT the roster: simply resuming a game in portrait. Rather than theorise again I instrumented the actual sequence under tag SF-CAM - surfaceCreated (with surface validity and whether a thread already exists), the double-init skip, GL init OK, GL init FAILED with the message, surfaceDestroyed, every probe strike with the isRendering value it saw, and the moment it announces. These ride in the ticket via DiagnosticLog.tail, which I also widened from 100 to 300 lines because the last ticket arrived with NO camera evidence at all - ordinary startup chatter had already pushed it out. ONE REGRESSION CANDIDATE worth checking against the log: EGLManager was narrowed on 2026-08-20 to request EGL_WINDOW_BIT only, and the comment justifying it asserts "on real hardware configs supporting both are plentiful, so this changes nothing" - an assumption about hardware, never measured on Eric device. If eglChooseConfig now returns a different config on his Samsung, genuine surface failures would be new exactly when he says they started. Shipped 1.1.1333.
ROOT CAUSE + FIXED 1.1.1334: TWO CameraEngines racing for one SurfaceAndroidPASSThe SF-CAM instrumentation paid for itself immediately. Ticket SF-2783GF, build 1.1.1333, Samsung SM-S948U: two surfaceCreated events 2ms apart, BOTH reporting existingThread=false - which proves they are different CameraEngine objects, because a second call on the same engine hits the double-init guard and logs SKIPPED. One then logged GL init FAILED: eglCreateWindowSurface failed, the other GL init OK 23ms later. A Surface can back only ONE EGLSurface, so the two engines were fighting over one SurfaceView: the winner drew the live preview Eric could see, the loser died, and MainActivity cameraEngine pointed at the LOSER - which is why the probe honestly read isRendering=false four times and announced a dead camera over a working picture. Two surfaceDestroyed at the end confirmed two live registrations. CAUSE: initializeCamera() is reachable from four call sites and each one constructed a fresh CameraEngine whose init does surfaceView.holder.addCallback(this), while release() never removed it - so callbacks accumulated permanently on one holder. FIX, both halves: release() now calls removeCallback(this) and shuts down its render thread, and initializeCamera() releases any existing engine before building a new one. This also explains the extra thermal warnings Eric mentioned in the same breath - the device was running two camera engines and two render threads, doing the work twice. Notably this is the THIRD two-instances bug of this run after the dual ScoreboardRenderer and the dual RemoteScoringServer; the shape is worth watching for. Shipped 1.1.1334.
fixed: CameraEngine.release() removeCallback + initializeCamera() idempotency
ticket SF-2783GF SF-CAM trace
Audited the leak CLASS after the dual-CameraEngine fix — comes back cleanAndroidPASSThe dual-CameraEngine bug was caused by a registration that accumulated: each initializeCamera() did surfaceView.holder.addCallback(this) and release() never removed it. Since that shape had already produced three bugs this run, I swept for it rather than assuming it was a one-off. Result: holder.addCallback appears EXACTLY ONCE in the entire codebase (CameraEngine:167) and now has its matching removeCallback (:892), so that specific leak is closed at the source rather than patched at a call site. Checked the next most suspicious candidate too - CameraHealthMonitor, which registers two system BroadcastReceivers on the Application (a host that outlives everything) - and it correctly unregisters both and removes its handler callbacks, so it is balanced; its lopsided add/remove count was an artifact of my crude grep. The remaining files with unbalanced counts (MainActivity, GamePageActivity, TeamSearchActivity and friends) are Activity-scoped listeners on views that die with their Activity, which is not the same hazard - reporting those as findings would be noise. Worth stating the distinction plainly for whoever reads this next: the dangerous pattern is a REPEATEDLY-CONSTRUCTED object registering on a LONGER-LIVED host (a SurfaceHolder, a singleton, a system service). Raw add-vs-remove counts do not identify it.
iOS parity audit for the dual-CameraEngine bug — structurally immuneiOSPASSThe parity rule says an Android fix gets checked on iOS the same session, and the matrix had just flagged iOS camera as the weakest coverage, so this was the obvious thing to look at. Result: iOS CANNOT hit the Android bug, for an architectural reason rather than luck. Android had four call sites that each did cameraEngine = CameraEngine(...) and registered another SurfaceHolder callback, with nothing removing the old one - so two engines ended up fighting over one Surface. iOS owns the camera through SwiftUI: ContentView holds @StateObject private var cameraManager, and StateObject(wrappedValue:) evaluates its autoclosure only on FIRST creation, so re-rendering the view cannot produce a second manager. Every other reference is @ObservedObject - passed in, never constructed. There is exactly one AVCaptureSession in the codebase, a private let inside CameraSession, and exactly one CameraSession(), held by that single manager. Also checked the accumulation pattern more broadly: NotificationCenter observers are net balanced (13 added, 16 removed), and the two files that add without removing are app-lifetime observers rather than per-instance ones. BE CLEAR ABOUT WHAT THIS IS: a code audit, not a behavioural test. It rules out this specific bug class on iOS; it does not close the camera coverage gap the matrix exposed (24 steps total, iOS reading not tested), which needs actual recording runs on a device.
CONFIRMED BY ERIC on 1.1.1334: camera dialog gone AND thermal warnings settledAndroidPASSEric on the real device: "seems to have fixed the dialog and the thermal issue." That closes the loop on both symptoms and, more usefully, validates the diagnosis rather than just the fix. The dialog going away only proves the announce path is quiet; the THERMAL warnings settling is the independent confirmation, because that was an inference not an observation - I predicted it from the mechanism (two CameraEngines meant two render threads and two camera pipelines, so the phone was doing all that work twice) rather than measuring it. Had the dialog stopped but the heat continued, the instance theory would have been wrong or incomplete. Both moving together is what a correct root cause looks like. Worth remembering that this arrived only after I stopped guessing: 1332 was a wrong fix shipped on a plausible model, and it took instrumenting the surface/GL lifecycle in 1333 and reading Eric SF-CAM trace to see two surfaceCreated events 2ms apart. PRODUCTION IS NOW UNBLOCKED - the roster crash (1331) was the stated blocker and the camera bug was the last open field report. Not pushing: the standing rule is that production is mine to run but his to trigger, per build, and he has confirmed a fix rather than given a go-ahead.
iOS camera coverage: the UI/lifecycle half IS testable on the sim, the FRAMES half is notiOSINFOScoping the 'camera coverage gap' properly: the matrix cell is Camera & recording x Android 40 vs iOS 9, and this is why. Recording START WORKS on the simulator and drives the whole state machine correctly - red dot + live timer, '1080p 30fps', '15.0 GB free' storage estimate, buttons swapping to PAUSE/STOP/⭐, the free-tier 'Remove watermark' banner, the highlight duration sheet (5/10/20s/Since last pause), and a Stop Recording confirmation that MATCHES Android's (Cancel/Stop). The file is created with the right name, and iOS's scheme matches Android's MainActivity.kt:31489 - SF_{sportAbbrev}_{shortId}_{date}_{seq} - so the SF_{shortId}_{NNN} line in CLAUDE.md is just stale. BUT the file is 0 BYTES: the sim has no camera, the debug HUD counts 'REC 0f' and the app ends with 'Recording empty'. Same wall the Android emulator hit before its fake camera worked. Good news is it FAILS HONESTLY rather than claiming a successful save. CONCLUSION: iOS camera coverage can be closed for UI, gating, lifecycle, filenames and storage/limit logic, and CANNOT be closed on the simulator for anything needing real frames - burned-in scoreboard, highlight extraction content, reels, outro mux. Those need Eric's iPhone, exactly as the Android side needed a real device.

Why iOS camera coverage CANNOT be closed on the sim: the recorder is push-driven by the cameraAndroid+iOSINFOTried the obvious trick: iOS already HAS Android's debug 'Sport Image' (MetalCompositor.debugMockTexture, `effectiveCamera = debugMockTexture ?? cameraTexture`, QS tile id debug_sport_image, key debug_sport_image_enabled, mocks for baseball/softball/bowling/volleyball). Enabled it via `simctl spawn <udid> defaults write com.scoreframe.ios debug_sport_image_enabled -bool YES` and the volleyball mock DID render in the preview. Recorded 20s anyway: still a 0-BYTE file. ROOT CAUSE - the two platforms drive the writer from opposite ends. iOS: `captureOutput(_:didOutput:from:)` on AVCaptureVideoDataOutput is the ONLY thing that feeds the recorder (CameraSession.swift:496). No AVCaptureDevice on a simulator means no callbacks, so zero frames are written no matter what the compositor paints for the preview - the mock substitutes the DISPLAY texture only, it does not synthesise sample buffers. Android: the GL render thread PULLS, drawing into the recording surface on its own cadence, so a mock texture lands in the encoded file too. That asymmetry - not effort or tooling - is why Camera & recording sits at Android 40 / iOS 9, and it will not move on a simulator. PROPOSAL FOR ERIC (deliberately NOT built tonight): make the iOS writer tick from a display link while a debug mock is active, strictly debug-gated. It would permanently close most of the iOS camera gap, but it edits a shipping recording path, which is not something to change unattended.
CameraSession.swift:496-499 vs CameraRenderer.kt:174-200; two 0-byte SF_VB_*.mp4 with the mock ON
iOS SIMULATOR NOW RECORDS REAL VIDEO — burned-in scoreboard verified from the file itselfiOSPASSEric approved building this. It turned out to need four lines, not a display-link rewrite. ROOT CAUSE was NOT 'the recorder is push-driven' as I wrote in trap 52 — that was half right and led me to the wrong shape of fix. The real blocker is in CameraView.draw(in:), which is ALREADY a display link and ALREADY substitutes a blank/debug-mock texture when there is no camera. The line that killed it: `guard pts != lastRecordedPTS` — a dedupe meant to skip duplicate camera frames at 60fps display / 30fps capture. On a simulator captureOutput never fires, so latestPresentationTime stays .invalid forever, pts never advances, and EVERY frame is dropped. The writer opens, names the file correctly, and closes it at 0 bytes. FIX: when pixelBuffer == nil, synthesise a 30fps PTS from a frame counter. Wrapped in `#if targetEnvironment(simulator)` so it is COMPILED OUT on device — the shipping recording path cannot be affected, which is stronger than a runtime debug flag. PROVEN FROM THE ARTEFACT, not the UI: recorded ~15s of volleyball tapping HOME twice, then probed the file with AVFoundation. 47s, one video track, 1080x1920 @30fps, and the frame pulled at 33s shows the mock court WITH the scoreboard burned in reading HOME 2 / AWAY 0 and 'SET 1 (to 25) | Best of 3 | Sets: 0-0' — the score matches the taps. UNLOCKS the whole class trap 52 called impossible: burned-in scoreboard, highlight clip contents, reels, outro mux — all testable headless. Camera & recording was Android 40 / iOS 9 precisely because of this.

CORRECTION: renderSportTexture is the DISPATCHER, not a board — 13 renderers, not 14iOSINFOI built the sweep plan by grepping `private func render*Texture` and treating every hit as a board. `renderSportTexture` is not one — it is the router: it checks simpleMode, then switches on sport to call the real renderers. Counting it inflated the target by one. The true count is 13 distinct boards. Same lesson as the softball mis-plan and the MultiBowlerBowling dead code: a grep gives you NAMES, not a model of what the code does. Check what each hit IS before planning work around it.
