TestPlatformDetail
iOS parity #1: ticket payload now carries app state (4 fields)iOSPASSFirst item of the iOS-to-1334 parity work, and it starts with a correction of my own. I grepped the iOS ticket payload too narrowly, saw only name/email/category/subject/body/platform/appVersion, and told Eric iOS sends no device, no crash stack and no logs. WRONG - iOS builds a separate debugInfo dictionary further down with device, iOS version, locale, free disk, signed-in state, uid, recentLogs AND lastCrashStack, backed by a real DiagnosticLog with an uncaught-exception handler installed at app start. Diffed the two payloads properly instead: Android 19 keys, iOS 11. Of the 10 Android-only keys, four are platform equivalents iOS already has under different names (androidSdk/androidRelease vs iosVersion, appVersionCode vs appBuildNumber) or have no iOS analogue (logcatDump). The genuine gap was APP STATE - what the user was doing - so I added currentSport, videoResolution, stabilization and hdr, deliberately reading the SAME UserDefaults keys Android uses so the two payloads read identically in the console. Left out zoomMode and recordingState: neither is reachable from the feedback view without new plumbing, and Android own recordingState came through as "unknown" in the real ticket anyway, so the honest move is to skip rather than fake them. Worth noting iOS never had the enum bug that blocked Android in-game tickets: GameSession.sport is already a String there. Builds clean.

RETRACTED — REAL parity bug found: scoringMode default differs across platformsAndroid+iOSINFOFound while investigating the fixture, and this one is genuine rather than a test artifact. When volleyball scoringMode is ABSENT from a wire message, Android decodes it as "simple" (RemoteScoringProtocol.kt:1393, optString default) and iOS decodes it as "advanced" (VolleyballState.swift:243). Identical message, two different interpretations - an Android controller shows Simple while an iOS controller shows Advanced for the same game. iOS choice is deliberate and documented (build 303: older saved games and pre-303 remote state keep their stats UI), and Android choice is the conservative one (do not show Pro stats UI to someone who may not have it). Both are defensible, which is exactly why I am NOT picking a side silently - it changes what users see, so it is Eric call. Note the field is present on all current messages from both platforms, so this only bites legacy saved games and cross-version pairs; but that is precisely the case a controller from an older build hits. —— RETRACTED: Apples-to-oranges: I compared ANDROID'S SOCCER decoder against IOS'S VOLLEYBALL decoder. Per sport they actually agree. SOCCER: Android RemoteScoringProtocol.kt:1393 defaults 'simple'; iOS SoccerState.swift:239 defaults 'simple' — identical. VOLLEYBALL: iOS VolleyballState.swift:243 defaults 'advanced'; Android's Volleyball data class has NO scoringMode field at all, and MainActivity.kt:28523 seeds VB mode from rememberedScoringMode('VOLLEYBALL', freeDefault = 'advanced') — also 'advanced'. No default mismatch exists on either sport. Eric asked 'why would the field be absent' and checking it properly to answer him is what exposed the error.
The REAL volleyball difference: Android never puts scoringMode on the wireAndroid+iOSINFOWhat is left after retracting the bogus default mismatch. The difference is STRUCTURAL, not a default: iOS carries volleyball scoringMode inside VolleyballState and encodes it in toJson, so it crosses the wire; Android keeps volleyball mode as local UI state (rememberedScoringMode) and its Volleyball data class has no such field, so it never crosses. CONSEQUENCE in a cross-platform pair: the two phones do not share which volleyball mode the game is in. An iOS camera in Advanced tells an Android controller nothing, and the controller falls back to its own remembered setting. They will usually agree by luck, because both default to 'advanced' — which is probably why this has never been reported. NOT filed as a bug pending Eric's call: adding the field to Android's Volleyball state is an additive protocol change (safe by the opt*() convention), but it changes what a controller shows mid-game, so it is a product decision rather than a defect.
ScoreboardType.kt Volleyball has no scoringMode; Soccer:236 does. iOS VolleyballState.swift:110/149 both declares and encodes it.
Volleyball scoringMode now crosses the wire on Android — verified by socket captureAndroid+iOSPASSEric approved 'add it to Android's wire'. Done and confirmed end to end. FIRST, A CORRECTION TO MY OWN EARLIER CLAIM: I said Android's Volleyball data class had NO scoringMode field. It does — added v1.1.681, with a comment mirroring iOS's word for word. My `grep -A 40` window stopped short of line 66 and I reported the false negative as fact. Same shape as the soccer/volleyball mix-up earlier: a truncated search window read as evidence of absence. THE REAL GAP, now fixed: the field existed on both platforms with the SAME default ('advanced'), but Android never serialized or deserialized it for volleyball — only for soccer. So a cross-platform pair agreed by coincidence, not by exchange. CHANGE: `put("scoringMode", state.scoringMode)` in the Volleyball serializer and `scoringMode = json.optString("scoringMode", "advanced")` in its decoder. Purely additive and default-read, per the protocol-v2 opt*() rule — an older peer that omits it decodes identically on either platform. VERIFIED: started an Advanced volleyball game on emulator-5554, put it in camera mode, attached a headless probe over `adb forward tcp:9876`. The ACK carries state.scoringMode='advanced'. OUTCOME side checked too rather than assumed: both panels gate on this exact field — VolleyballScoringPanel.swift:81 and MainActivity.kt:13876 — so a receiver now renders the camera's mode instead of its own remembered one.
CONNECTION_ACK from an Android VB camera: sport='VOLLEYBALL', state.scoringMode='advanced'
renderMultiBowlerBowlingTexture is DEAD CODE on iOS — Android shows the stacked board, iOS never doesiOSBUGThe narrow, real finding the sweep turned up. iOS HAS a multi-bowler stacked renderer — ~430 lines of it — and nothing calls it. Its own early-returns for bowlerCount <= 1 are the only references. CONSEQUENCE: on Android the stacked view appears in the MAGNIFIER and the END-GAME overlay (createMultiBowlerScoreboard, per CLAUDE.md). On iOS the magnifier reads back compositor.scoreboardTexture — literally the same texture as the video — so it shows the single active bowler too. An iOS user bowling with 3 people can never see all three grids anywhere; an Android user can. NOT a video-overlay bug: both platforms deliberately keep the live overlay single-bowler. It is a missing surface. Small, self-contained, and probably why it was never noticed — worth Eric's call on whether the magnifier should stack like Android's.
ScoreboardTextureCache.swift:3292 declared; grep finds no call site anywhere in ScoreFrame/. generateScoreboardZoomImage reads compositor.scoreboardTexture, i.e. the same single-bowler texture the video uses.
FIXED: iOS magnifier now shows the STACKED multi-bowler board — dead renderer wired upiOSPASSEric's call was to wire the dead renderer to BOTH the magnifier and the end-game overlay. MAGNIFIER: DONE. Added ScoreboardTextureCache.zoomTexture(for:sport:videoWidth:) which routes to renderMultiBowlerBowlingTexture for bowling with >1 bowler and otherwise returns exactly the texture the video uses; generateScoreboardZoomImage prefers it. Deliberately NOT routed through the video dispatch — both platforms keep the burned-in overlay on the single active bowler by design. A false alarm on the way, worth recording: my first check showed a SINGLE board and looked like the fix had failed. It had not — I opened the magnifier on a freshly-started game before any roll, and `genericState` is only repopulated on a state change (ContentView.swift:10302), so it still held a default ('PLAYER 1', no allBowlers). One roll later the stacked board appeared. SIDE FINDING: that means the magnifier on a brand-new game, before the first scoring action, shows a default board rather than the real one. Pre-existing, minor, not filed.
3-bowler game; magnifier shows three stacked frame grids. evidence/ios_burnin/bowling_multi_magnifier.png
Multi-bowler END-GAME half NOT done — iOS's end-game overlay is a different artifactAndroid+iOSINFOEric asked for magnifier AND end-game. The magnifier is done; the end-game half is NOT, and it is not plumbing — the two platforms build genuinely different things there. ANDROID burns the STACKED SCOREBOARD itself into the last seconds of video (showEndGameOverlay -> createMultiBowlerScoreboard). IOS burns a SUMMARY CARD built by EndGameOverlayBuilder — FINAL banner, team boxes, one stat line; for bowling that line is just 'FRAMES n'. There is no scoreboard in it at all. So 'add the stacked grid to iOS's end-game' means either replacing the card for bowling, or compositing a tall frame grid into a card that was not designed to hold one. That is a design decision about what an iOS bowling end-game should LOOK like, not a missing call, so I am asking rather than picking.
Android MainActivity.kt:21063 showEndGameOverlay -> createMultiBowlerScoreboard. iOS EndGameOverlayBuilder.swift is a 154-line summary card; its BOWLING case returns 'FRAMES n'.

CHECKED, NOT A BUG: bowling's pin pad disables the numerals after a 9iOSINFONearly filed this as 'the pin pad is dead'. After rolling a 9 the numeric keys stopped responding and every visible key looked greyed out, right after a run of genuine bugs — a tempting shape. Scrolling the panel showed the rest of the pad and the answer: with 9 pins down the only legal outcomes are 0 or 1, which bowling expresses as '-' (miss) and '/' (spare), and those two were enabled the whole time. The numerals and X are disabled precisely BECAUSE they would be illegal. Taking the spare re-enabled 1-9 and X for frame 2 and disabled '/'. The gating is correct in both directions. Reinforces trap 44/50: when a control 'does nothing', check that you can see the whole control before concluding anything — the enabled keys were below the fold.
Screenshots ca6-ca9: after a 9 on roll 1, keys 1-9 and X are dimmed; '-' and '/' remain enabled

OPEN — Eric: roster cross-contamination between controller and iOS cameraAndroid+iOSBUGReported alongside the resume failure and NOT yet investigated. Controlling an iOS camera from an Android controller: the CONTROLLER displayed the right volleyball roster — one player, CT Bulldogs — while the CAMERA's own scoring area showed a stale roster from a DIFFERENT team (PVA). So the two phones disagreed about who is on the court. Worth noting the shape matches the standing remote-scoring rule in CLAUDE.md: 'where does this data live?' — a camera reading its own local store instead of the roster that arrived over the wire, or holding a previous game's roster. The wire clearly carried the right data, since the controller rendered it correctly. Next step is to find which side is stale and whether the camera is failing to adopt the synced roster or is re-reading a cached one.
Eric, live 2026-08-24: controller showed the correct CT Bulldogs roster (1 player); the iOS camera's scoring area showed an older PVA roster.
Roster contamination — mechanism identified (HYPOTHESIS, not yet reproduced)iOSINFOExplains Eric's split — controller right (CT Bulldogs), camera wrong (PVA) — without guessing. WHY THE TWO PHONES DISAGREE: rosterFor() prefers the LOCAL saved team when `teamId` resolves, and only falls back to the wire roster otherwise. A CONTROLLER has never heard of the camera's teams, so it always takes the wire copy — which is correct. The CAMERA resolves its teamId locally and that wins. So a STALE teamId on the camera shows a stale roster while the wire data is fine. That is exactly the observed shape. WHERE THE STALE ID COMES FROM: team identity is written CONDITIONALLY. `applyGameSetupConfig` does `if let h = config.homeTeamId { self.homeTeamId = h }` and only updates the GameSession `if config.homeTeamId != nil`. The comment says so outright: 'nil means unchanged (user picked No Stats or sheet had no team pickers)'. So choosing No Stats — or any path yielding nil — LEAVES THE PREVIOUS GAME'S TEAM ID in both CameraManager and the persisted session. On resume, line 5631 faithfully restores that stale id from the session. (The fresh-game path at 5582 assigns unconditionally and is fine.) WHY IT NEEDS A REPRO BEFORE A FIX: nil legitimately means 'don't touch' for sheets with no pickers, so I cannot simply clear on nil without knowing which call path Eric hit. Three-state handling (untouched / assigned / explicitly none) is likely needed, and that is a semantic change to a shipping path. NEXT: reproduce in the lab — team A game, then a second game with No Stats, and see whether team A's roster follows.
VolleyballScoringPanel.rosterFor:687-699; ContentView.swift:9008 + 9013-9017 (conditional writes); 5582 (new game, unconditional) vs 5631 (resume, from session)
FIXED (iOS 1.2.244/714) — REPRODUCED: selecting 'No Stats' does not clear the team — the previous team leaks into the gameiOSBUGEric's roster contamination, reproduced from a clean slate rather than inferred. SETUP: created a volleyball team 'PVA' with one player (#77 PvaPlayer) on a sim whose active session had NO team ids. STEP 1 — in-game Setup, assign Home = PVA, Save. Session now has homeTeamId=55D93240-B257-42E4-96CC-A694E3CD1363. Correct. STEP 2 — reopen Setup, switch Home back to 'Home Team (No Stats)', Save. Session STILL has homeTeamId=55D93240-... The explicit choice of 'no team' did not clear it. STEP 3 — the leak is user-visible, not just on disk: the home score block now renders 'PVA' in a game whose Home team is set to No Stats. MECHANISM (matches the earlier hypothesis exactly): applyGameSetupConfig writes `if let h = config.homeTeamId { self.homeTeamId = h }` and only touches the session `if config.homeTeamId != nil`. nil is overloaded to mean BOTH 'the sheet had no picker' and 'the user explicitly chose no team', so the second case can never clear. From there VolleyballScoringPanel.rosterFor() resolves the stale id against the LOCAL TeamManager and local wins over the wire copy — which is why Eric's CONTROLLER (no local team, so it uses the wire) was right while the CAMERA was wrong. FIX NEEDS A DECISION: distinguishing the two nil meanings is a semantic change on a shipping path — GameSetupConfig has to carry 'untouched' vs 'explicitly none'. Not shipping that unilaterally after last night.
fixed: GameSetupConfig gains teamSelectionIsAuthoritative; GameSetupSheet sets it (it always shows both pickers, so index 0 genuinely means no team); applyGameSetupConfig assigns straight through when set — including nil — and mirrors the clear into the persisted GameSession so a resume cannot restore the stale id.
Pro Max sim: session homeTeamId stayed 55D93240-...-A694E3CD1363 (PVA) after switching Home to 'Home Team (No Stats)'; score block then rendered 'PVA'

Implemented and verified against the SAME failing state that reproduced the bug — the session still carried the stale PVA id from before the fix, so this is a true before/after on identical data. After choosing 'No Stats' and saving, homeTeamId is nil. That is the tie that rosterFor() resolves against TeamManager, so the wrong-roster symptom is addressed at its source. WHAT I DID NOT VERIFY, stated rather than assumed: the panel was in Simple mode, so no roster row was on screen to watch change. The id clearing is the mechanism; I have not seen the pills themselves swap. SEPARATE BEHAVIOUR WORTH ERIC'S EYE: the score block STILL reads 'PVA' after the clear. The team NAME lives independently of the team tie — assigning PVA renamed home, and choosing No Stats does not rename it back. That is arguably correct (his option 2, 'clear the tie, keep the name', which he did NOT pick) but it is not obviously what he expects from 'clear the team'. #29 order Managing/Following by activity -- STILL REAL, premise confirmedandroidINFOConfirmed still real, and the task's premise (needs a per-hub cloud lookup) is correct. HomeDataStore's per-hub games listener DELIBERATELY excludes completed games: HomeDataStore.kt:283-286 filters whereIn(status, [scheduled, live, cancelled, postponed]) with an explicit comment that streaming full games/ would push every completed-game change and is wasteful. So last-game date is genuinely not in hand. FollowedHubItem (HomeDataStore.kt:422-428) carries only profile / upcomingGames / isLive / nextGame / isManager -- no past-activity stamp. TEMPTING BUT WRONG shortcut: HubProfile.updatedAt (TeamHubManager.kt:45) is already in HomeData at zero cost, but it measures the wrong thing -- games live in the teams/{teamId}/games SUBcollection, so saving a game does not touch the parent team doc and updatedAt would not move when a followed team actually plays. CHEAPEST CORRECT PATH: one extra per-hub one-shot query (not a listener, preserving the bounded payload design) -- whereEqualTo(status, completed).orderBy(date, DESCENDING).limit(1) -- cached onto FollowedHubItem as lastGameAt. One doc read per hub per refresh. GOTCHA: needs a composite index on games (status ASC, date DESC). Removed COACH keeps the team forever -- removeCoach never strips myTeamsbothBUGROOT CAUSE of the symptom #24 was filed for, and it is worse than the task states -- it does not heal on restart. Chain: acceptInvitation with role=coach sets isStaff=true and arrayUnions the teamId into users/{uid}.myTeams (Android TeamHubManager.kt:1143-1167). But MembershipWriter.removeCoach only (a) deletes teams/{teamId}/coaches/{uid} and (b) arrayRemoves 'coach' from users/{uid}/memberships/{teamId}.roles. It NEVER arrayRemoves myTeams (Android MembershipWriter.kt:70-92, iOS MembershipWriter.swift:54-69 -- identical on both). removeManager DOES strip myTeams, which is why the manager case looks fixed and the coach case does not. And nothing closes the gap downstream: NO client on either platform listens to the memberships subcollection -- grep shows it is written by MembershipWriter and never read or observed -- so the role removal has no live-refresh path at all. CONSEQUENCE: the revoked coach keeps the team and its full roster on-device permanently (not 'until they kill and reopen it'), it keeps showing in My Teams, and the app keeps five live Firestore listeners attached to a team the user no longer has rights on. Whether those listeners now error out under the roles rules is a separate question I have not tested. FIX: add the myTeams arrayRemove to removeCoach on both platforms, mirroring removeManager. NOT YET APPLIED -- only the main session writes code.
Android MembershipWriter.kt:70-92 vs TeamHubManager.kt:1432-1448 + :1143-1167; iOS MembershipWriter.swift:54-69 vs TeamHubManager.swift:1102-1116; no memberships listener in either RealtimeSyncManager
FIXED — removeCoach now releases myTeams (both platforms, conditional)bothPASSThe bug found in triage is fixed. New shared helper releaseMyTeamsIfNoStaffLeft(teamId, uid) on both platforms; removeCoach calls it after the batch commits. IT IS CONDITIONAL ON PURPOSE, and that half matters as much as the release: staff roles are independent sibling subcollections, so one person can be manager AND coach. An unconditional arrayRemove would evict someone who still holds the other role -- which is a SECOND bug I found while fixing the first: removeManager was already doing exactly that unconditionally. Both now route through the same helper, which checks managers/{uid} and coaches/{uid} and releases only when the last staff role is gone. Ordering is load-bearing: the helper runs AFTER the role doc is deleted, or the check sees the very doc being removed. Best-effort by design -- a failure cannot fail a role removal that has already committed. VERIFIED: Android :app:compileDebugKotlin clean; iOS xcodebuild Release BUILD SUCCEEDED (warnings pre-existing, no errors). NOT verified against live Firestore -- no revoked-coach account to test with, so the behaviour change is code-verified only.
fixed: Android MembershipWriter.kt + TeamHubManager.kt; iOS MembershipWriter.swift

Transient 'Connected + borrowed tier' MEASURED -- 8-13s, and only on a SILENT deathandroidBUGReproduced in the lab against 1.1.1335 with fake_camera.py, and the shape is narrower than the backlog item assumed. TWO different failure modes, only one of which is slow: (1) CLEAN death -- camera process exits, socket sends EOF. RemoteScoringClient logs 'Server disconnected (EOF)' and ConnectionManager releases within ~4s. Measured 4.0s and 4.2s across two runs; SF-PRO logs 'released borrowed tier'. Healthy. (2) SILENT death -- socket stays OPEN but the camera never replies (Wi-Fi drop, camera phone asleep). Simulated with SIGSTOP on the fake camera. Measured: at 2.6s and 7.0s the UI still said 'Connected to Camera Phone' AND still applied the borrowed tier; recovery at 12.8s. That matches MAX_MISSED_HEARTBEATS(4) x HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_MS(2000) = 8s plus repaint latency. SO: the window where the app asserts a connection that no longer exists, and applies a tier borrowed from a phone that is gone, is roughly 8-13 SECONDS, and only on a silent death. Eric's report ('it thought I was still Pro but it also thought I was still connected') is this state. RECOMMENDED FIX (not applied): surface 'Reconnecting...' rather than 'Connected' as soon as missedHeartbeats >= 1, and hold the borrowed tier as provisional until a reply arrives. The client already tracks missedHeartbeats (RemoteScoringClient.kt:60) -- it simply is not exposed to the UI, so the honest state is known internally and never shown.
fake_camera SIGSTOP run: t=2.6s conn=True tier=Free; t=7.0s conn=True; t=12.8s conn=False tier=Pro
Borrowed-tier lifecycle is CORRECT in both directions -- verified on the wireandroidPASSChecked while investigating the transient, and worth recording on its own because the borrowed-Pro feature cost days earlier in this run. Against a fake camera advertising recorderIsPro=false, on an emulator whose OWN account is Pro: the controller adopted the camera's FREE tier (SF-PRO 'adopted recorderIsPro=false -> isPro=false') and the bottom-nav tier label correctly read 'Free' while paired. On disconnect it released (SF-PRO 'released borrowed tier') and the label returned to 'Pro' -- the user's own subscription came back, verified at 4.2s. So borrowing works in BOTH directions and does not strand a paying user on Free after an encounter with a free camera. METHOD NOTE: my first attempt measured the Pro direction and appeared to show borrowed Pro persisting forever. That was WRONG -- the emulator's own account is Pro (debug_tier_override=billing, so no debug forcing), which meant the label was going to read 'Pro' either way. Testing the OPPOSITE direction was what made the result readable. Same trap that consumed days earlier this run: a tier reading proves nothing unless you know the BASE tier it is resolving against.
SF-PRO adopted/released logs; nav label Free while paired -> Pro at 4.2s after loss
iOS transient window is the SAME 8s -- and iOS says 'Connected' while the dot is rediosBUGCode-verified parity check on the transient measured on Android. CONSTANTS ARE IDENTICAL: RemoteScoringClient.swift:233-234 heartbeatIntervalMs=2000, maxMissedHeartbeats=4 -- exactly Android's RemoteScoringClient.kt:22-23. So the stale window is the same ~8s by construction, and for the same reason: neither platform surfaces missedHeartbeats before it crosses the threshold, so for four heartbeats the UI keeps asserting a live connection. THE RELEASE PATH IS SOUND, and cleaner than Android's: timeout -> internalDisconnect() (:376) -> teardownConnection() -> clearBorrowedTier() (:431) sets sessionProOverride=nil, and FeatureGate.sessionProOverride has a didSet that posts .featureGateChanged (FeatureGate.swift:153-158), so observing views repaint automatically -- no manual refresh, unlike Android's bottom nav which is built once. THE iOS-SPECIFIC GAP: after the timeout iOS sets connectionState = .reconnecting (RemoteScoringClient.swift:496) and NO VIEW READS IT. The controller header (ControllerScreen.swift:84-87) flips only the status DOT green->red, while the text stays `connectedSport?.capitalized ?? "Connected"`. So a dropped iOS controller shows a RED DOT next to the word Connected (or the sport name) and never says Reconnecting. Arguably worse than Android, which at least removes its Connected row. CHEAPER TO FIX THAN ANDROID: iOS already has the state to drive an honest label (connectionState == .reconnecting); Android would first have to expose missedHeartbeats. SCOPE NOTE, stated plainly: this is READ FROM THE CODE, not measured on a device. The empirical 8-13s measurement was taken on Android; iOS shares the constants and an equivalent timeout->teardown path, but I did not run the SIGSTOP experiment against a simulator.

'Take Over' + Setup/End pills together means DISCONNECTED, not taken-overandroidPASSEric asked whether the camera phone showing Game Setup / End Game next to a 'Take Over' button meant the controller-always rule had broken. It has not -- the rule is intact and the two things are consistent. 'Take Over' lives in showDisconnectedStatusBar() (MainActivity.kt:41630), which also renders a warning dot, the literal text 'Controller disconnected', and a 'Reconnect' button. It has exactly ONE call site (:39930). So the button can only appear once the controller has DROPPED -- never while connected. And dropping sets controllerDisconnectedDuringGame = true, which makes cameraWithControllerPresent false (:12783), which is precisely what brings the three pills back (:12785-12787). Pills + Take Over together therefore reads 'controller disconnected, camera is driving solo', not 'someone already took over'. The hide-when-controlled rule still gates on !controllerDisconnectedDuringGame && !hasTakenOverFromController. ON THE NO-OP INSTINCT, which was half right: the PILL half of Take Over is redundant by the time you can press it -- the disconnect already restored them. What it still does: sets hasTakenOverFromController (permanent, survives state resets, so a reconnecting controller cannot take the pills away again), stops the TCP server AND the Firebase relay, sets role NONE, clears isRemoteOnlyMode/isServerMode, and removes the server toggle row. So it is 'make solo permanent and tear down the server', not a no-op -- but the visible change is nil, which is exactly why it reads as one.

BUILT (both platforms) — 'Reconnecting…' instead of a false 'Connected'bothPASSEric approved shipping this. MY OWN BUG, caught on the emulator — a 1-miss trigger fires on EVERY healthy beatbothBUGFirst cut triggered at missedHeartbeats >= 1. Wrong, and only visible by running it: the counter is incremented immediately after SENDING a heartbeat and reset when the ACK returns, so on a perfectly healthy link it oscillates 0 -> 1 -> 0 every two seconds. A >= 1 test is therefore true for a slice of every beat -- Android would have fired onReconnecting() twice a second, and the iOS header would have strobed 'Reconnecting…' on a good connection. That is a worse lie than the one the change exists to fix, and it would have gone out looking like a flaky connection rather than a UI bug. Now 2 consecutive misses (~4s): still half the old 8s blackout, and unreachable while the camera is replying. 'Reconnecting…' is recoverable copy, so erring 2s late costs nothing while erring early is visible noise in every game.
fixed: threshold raised to 2 consecutive misses
fake_camera run on emulator-5554; heartbeat loop increments before ACK

MY SECOND BUG — announcing staleness through onReconnecting() TORE DOWN the connectionandroidBUGThis is why last cycle's emulator run never stabilised, and it was my change, not a fake-camera limitation as I guessed at the time. ConnectionManager.keepAliveClientCallback.onReconnecting() (:1254) deliberately treats that callback as TERMINAL -- it logs 'server unreachable — treating as disconnected' and calls cleanUpClientConnection(). onReconnecting() is fired from handleConnectionLost, so that is correct for its real meaning. Reusing it to announce a couple of unanswered heartbeats therefore DROPPED A PERFECTLY RECOVERABLE SESSION ~1.5s after connecting. FIX: a new ClientCallback.onLinkStale(isStale: Boolean) with a DEFAULT NO-OP body -- purely informational, never lifecycle. Default body means the five existing implementers (MainActivity x2, ConnectionManager, RemoteDiscoveryActivity x3) compile untouched and surfaces that do not care about link quality are unaffected. Recovery also routes through onLinkStale(false) rather than onReconnected(), because the latter is the full recovery path (repaints the bar, REBUILDS THE SCORING PANEL, re-syncs state) and firing that for a two-beat hiccup would rebuild the panel mid-game under the user. Verified fixed: the connection now holds with heartbeats flowing where it previously died in 1.5s.
fixed: new informational ClientCallback.onLinkStale(isStale)
ConnectionManager.kt:1254; RemoteScoringClient.kt onLinkStale; before: teardown at 1.5s, after: stable

'Reconnecting…' COMPLETE on both platforms, each verified on a devicebothPASSEric's queue item 2 is finished and both halves are demonstrated rather than argued. Android 1.1.1337: bar goes 'Connected (Wi-Fi)' -> 'Reconnecting…' at 2.6s -> back to 'Connected (Wi-Fi)' on resume. iOS 1.2.246/716: header goes 'Basketball' -> 'Reconnecting…' at 4.5s. Both replaced an 8-13s stretch where the UI asserted a connection that no longer existed. Two bugs of my own were caught along the way, both only visible by running it: a 1-miss threshold that strobed on healthy links, and announcing staleness through onReconnecting(), which the Home keep-alive treats as terminal and used to tear the session down. REMAINING GAP, stated plainly: the Android HOME screen row still reads 'Connected to Camera Phone' during the stale window -- onLinkStale is implemented in MainActivity (the scoring screen), while Home uses ConnectionManager's keepAliveClientCallback, which takes the default no-op. Same fix shape if Eric wants it there too.

RETRACTED my own plan — removing iOS 'Undo Last' would be a REGRESSION, not a portiosINFOI listed 'rename/move Undo across five panels' as a straightforward port in the last RESUME NEXT. That was WRONG and I caught it before executing. The two undos cover DIFFERENT actions on iOS: * undoLastBasketballStat() pops statLog and reverses per-player stats, points and fouls -- STAT PILL taps only. iOS has exactly ONE statLog.append (BasketballScoringViewModel.swift:251) and no sentinel for unattributed scores. * undo() pops a full-state history stack pushed from NINE call sites -- addPoints (which is what the BIG scoring row calls, BasketballScoringPanel:258), nextPeriod, setPeriod, setUseQuarters, addFoul, togglePossession, useTimeout, subtractPoints, resetGame. So deleting the 'Undo Last' pill would leave iOS with NO way to undo a direct +1 from the big scoring row, a foul, a timeout, a possession toggle or a period change. WHY ANDROID'S REMOVAL WAS SAFE and this is not: Android did the work FIRST. MainActivity.kt:15199 says it plainly -- 'The box-score undo walks the stat log and NOW REVERSES UNATTRIBUTED SCORES TOO, so it covers every case.' Android's v1.1.703 unified undo pushes a '_point' sentinel onto statUndoStack for plain +1 taps (MainActivity.kt:14258-14275). iOS never got that. CORRECT ORDER: (1) port the '_point' sentinel so iOS's box-score undo reverses unattributed scores; (2) THEN remove 'Undo Last' from the five panels. Doing (2) alone ships a regression. Not attempted -- (1) is a scoring-correctness change and deserves daylight, not an overnight run.

scoring-correctness 1 pass

TestPlatformDetail
FIXED (both platforms) — undoing a PLAYER foul no longer eats a TEAM foulbothPASSEric: 'go ahead and fix the foul thing if you figure it out.' Figured out and fixed. THE DEFECT: undo's PF branch decremented the TEAM foul count, but tapping the player 'Foul' pill never incremented it -- applyStat/bumpBasketballStat mutate only that player's own foul counter. The team count is moved solely by the FOUL tile. So every undo of a player foul silently removed a team foul that was never added, walking the count down over a game and flipping the BONUS indicator off early. IT WAS ON BOTH PLATFORMS -- iOS BasketballScoringViewModel and Android MainActivity:16222 had the identical asymmetry. A shared pre-existing bug, not a divergence, so both got the same fix: the rollback now applies only to an actual TEAM foul entry (iOS TEAM_PF; on Android the block is gone, since its team foul tile logs nothing and was never undoable). LEFT FOR ERIC, deliberately: in real basketball a personal foul DOES count toward the team total, and the rollback reads like the original author assumed it did. If that is the intent, the fix runs the OTHER way -- make the player Foul pill increment team fouls. That changes how scoring accrues, so it is a product decision rather than something to slip in while repairing an undo. Both files carry that note at the fix site.
fixed: undo's team-foul rollback restricted to team fouls only
BasketballScoringViewModel.swift TEAM_PF-only branch; MainActivity.kt:16222 block removed

METHOD BLOCKER — the two lab devices are on DIFFERENT TIERS, so panel diffs are invalidharnessBUGFound on the first sport, before reporting anything as a gap. The Android emulator resolves to PRO (debug_tier_override=billing, and the nav shows 'Pro'); the iOS simulator is FREE (it opens the Pro upsell sheet). Feature availability therefore differs BY CONFIGURATION, not by platform. Concretely: the Android volleyball panel shows the whole Stats block -- 'Stats' label, player chips (#1 Ana / #2 Bree / #3 Cara), Kill/Ace/Asst/Dig/Blk/Err/Miss for BOTH teams, Undo + History, Edit Roster -- and the iOS panel shows none of it. That reads like a huge parity gap and is almost certainly just the tier: per-player volleyball stats are Pro-gated on iOS (VolleyballScoringPanel carries an explicit 'Per-player stats need Pro on this device' branch), and E2E Spikers also has an empty roster. So NOTHING is being filed as a volleyball gap from this pass. Equalise first. HOW TO EQUALISE next session: either set the iOS debug tier override to Pro, or set Android's to Force FREE. NOTE the iOS control is on Home under MORE and my swipes did not reach it -- the vertical page would not scroll because the swipe landed on the horizontally-scrolling Recent Games carousel. Swipe on a column with no horizontal scroller (e.g. x near the left margin), or drive it from Settings instead.
Android nav 'Pro' + debug_tier_override=billing; iOS shows Pro upsell; evidence/parity/vb_android.png vs vb_ios.png

Tier + roster EQUALISED — and it dissolved the scary-looking volleyball 'gap'harnessPASSSet Android's debug_tier_override from 'billing' to 'free' (written into ScoreFrameSettings.xml via run-as with the app force-stopped, then CONFIRMED through the UI: the nav tier label now reads 'Free', per the trap-66 rule about not trusting a fixture the app has not acknowledged). Seeded E2E Spikers with the SAME three players Android's VB Test has (#1 Ana, #2 Bree, #3 Cara), into the re-resolved container. Result: on Android at Free the ENTIRE Stats block vanishes -- no player chips, no Kill/Ace/Asst/Dig/Blk/Err/Miss -- exactly matching iOS. So last cycle's alarming difference was 100% the Pro gate and 0% parity, which is why it was held rather than filed. An AdMob test banner also appears on Android at Free, further confirming the tier actually changed.
evidence/parity/vb_android_free.png vs vb_ios_free.png; nav label 'Free'
REAL GAP — iOS has no Serve-indicator or Fire-icon toggle; Android doesiosBUGFirst genuine finding from the sweep, and it survived code verification rather than resting on a screenshot. Android's volleyball header carries two checkbox+icon toggle pairs at top-right -- 'Serve' and '🔥 Fire' (CLAUDE.md documents both; MainActivity.kt:13670 reads vb.showFireIcon and :13709 writes it from the checkbox). They are NOT Pro-gated: still present with the tier forced to Free. iOS HONOURS both flags when rendering -- VolleyballScoringPanel:422/461 gate the serve icon on showServeIndicator, and :866 gates the fire icon on showFireIcon && streak>=5 -- but NOTHING ON iOS EVER WRITES THEM. A repo-wide grep for assignments finds only the read-defaults in ScoreboardTextureCache.swift:951-952. So an iOS user can never turn the serve indicator or the fire streak icon on or off. SHARPER CONSEQUENCE worth noting: these flags travel over the remote-scoring wire, so an iOS phone paired to an Android camera inherits whatever Android set and still has no way to change it -- the setting is visible in its effects but unreachable.
MainActivity.kt:13670,13709 vs VolleyballScoringPanel.swift:422,461,866; no iOS writer
NOT a gap — iOS has set History, but reaches it differentlyiosINFOAndroid shows an always-present 'History' button beside Undo. iOS looked like it had nothing. It does: VolleyballScoringPanel has a `setHistory` section (:840) rendered at :122-125 -- but guarded on `!viewModel.state.homeScoreBySet.isEmpty`, so it only appears once at least one set has finished. At 0-0 there is nothing to show and nothing is shown. So this is an ACCESS difference, not a missing feature: Android offers a button that presumably opens an empty-state dialog, iOS inlines the section once it has content. Recording it as a difference for Eric to rule on rather than filing a bug against iOS.
VolleyballScoringPanel.swift:122-125,840; Android History button in vb_android_free.png
BOWLING — at parity; nothing to fixbothPASSSecond sport through the equalised recipe (both devices forced to Free, same setup path). Matching on both: the 'Bowler 1' header with score + 'Series 0' + 'Max 300' on the right, the 'Frame 1 · Roll 1' line, the horizontally-scrolling ten-frame strip with frame 1 highlighted, and the numeric pad. The special keys are present on BOTH -- Fault, Split, Clear, X, / and - (on iOS they sit below the fold in portrait, which is why a screenshot alone suggested they were missing; the accessibility dump found all six). Pro gating also matches: bowler counts 4/5/6 show the lock on both platforms at Free, which independently confirms the tier really did change on Android. TWO COSMETIC DIFFERENCES, neither worth a fix on its own: the special keys are ALL-CAPS on Android and Title Case on iOS; and the bowler-name accent renders orange on Android vs blue on iOS. The latter is NOT the sport accent drifting -- both platforms define bowling purple #A855F7 identically (ScoreFrameUI.swift:335, ScoreFrameUI.kt:283) -- so it is the per-bowler colour defaulting differently. Flagging, not chasing.

Test automation — where it actually stands

Web pages — working, and now the way things get checked New today

A real browser signed in as a chosen account, driving scoreframe.app. Screenshots, element probes, and layout measurement. This is how the roster order was confirmed (#1 Maebry → #10 Arya, your order) and how the desktop layout was measured at 760px centred rather than eyeballed.

It matters because the previous habit was fetching the HTML and grepping for a string, which proves a file deployed and nothing else. Twice yesterday I "verified" CSS that referenced selectors matching no element on the page.

iOS simulator — never actually blocked. I misread it My error

I reported this blocked on a permissions problem. It wasn't. Element dumps go to the full log; I was reading the runner's filtered output, saw nothing, and concluded the test harness couldn't see the app. The dump proves it could.

The real failure was mine twice over: my test used Android's role-picker label, and I had only read the Android half of the automation notes — the iOS half documents that exact flow, and the helper that handles it. No permission from you was ever needed.

Android emulator — working

Driven end to end as a brand-new user: account creation, team creation, roster edits, removal. That run found three bugs in my own roster work that no unit test could reach, and reproduced the untracked-removal case to prove 1224 fixed it. 88 unit tests, zero failures.

Backend — the strongest of the three

Security rules tested as real signed-in users over the REST API, because the admin SDK bypasses rules and proves nothing about them: 17/17, including that an approved follower of a public team is refused surnames. The self-approve hole was both found and confirmed-closed this way.

To do — not started

Offer the other teams a player is on, when someone follows them You asked today

After they pick a relationship, ask whether they also want to request to follow the same player's other teams, with tick boxes, and send those requests to those owners. Needs care: the list of teams a child is on is exactly the aggregation we protect elsewhere, so it can only be offered once that person is an approved family member of the player — not while they are merely asking.

Create a player without creating a team

No way to add a player on their own, and no single screen holding everything about them. Confirmed today that birth month and year are NOT wanted there.

Players managing themselves Design first

The end state you described: a player with their own account who owns their name. Blocked on a decision rather than on code — a child registering makes ScoreFrame a service collecting information from children, which is a compliance programme, not a feature flag.

Edit-feature clips aren't tied to a player

Stat-driven reels index against the player they're of; clips made with the edit feature upload with no player attached, so they never appear in a player's list.

Cross-device roster resurrection

A second device still holding a removed player can re-upload them: roster membership has no tombstone, unlike deleted players.

Create a player without creating a team You asked · not started

There's no way to add a player on their own, and no single screen holding everything about them. That's where the extra player detail belongs, and where you'd build a player up before a team exists.

Edit-feature clips aren't tied to a player

Stat-driven reels index against the player they're of; clips made with the edit feature upload with no player attached, so they don't appear in a player's video list.

Cross-team match requests have no in-app screen

A guardian is asked on the web dashboard and by email. The plumbing is done and tested; there's no equivalent surface in the app yet.

Cross-device roster resurrection

A second device still holding a removed player re-uploads them on its next sync — roster membership has no tombstone, unlike deleted players. Same shape as the "Greg" bug.

App Check Do before the enforcement work

Ends the automated signups, and makes "a modified client" stop being a realistic threat — which is most of what the two items below defend against. Attestation on Android/iOS/web, then a monitor-first rollout so old builds aren't locked out.

The server still trusts the app about Pro Biggest item left

createTeamHub believes the client's isPro. Closing it means validating receipts against the Play Developer API and App Store Server API, plus a tier field only the server can write. Touches billing — do it properly.

hubIndex hijack rule 20 minutes, waiting on adoption

Written, with its deploy procedure in the rules file. Needs 1187+ adoption; 1197 only reached production yesterday. Check in a few days. The only security hole on the list.

Revive isn't server-enforced

Sharing is now. Reviving an archived team just flips a status field, so a modified client can revive past the cap. One callable mirroring createTeamHub.

Manage Team / Manage Player restyle Tabled

First pass matched Manage Game's button and not its composition, which is why it didn't look right. Three screens, two platforms — needs a session where the console layout is read end to end first.

2 months for the price of 1 Only revenue item on the board

Pay up front, then two months — supported by both stores as a "pay up front" introductory offer. Console setup first, app changes second. Still unstarted.

Shipped 12 Aug — to production

bothTeams made before signing in were being lost — they only uploaded if you happened to sign in from the Home screen. From the Account tab, the obvious place, they stayed on the phone and invisible forever, because team lists read from the cloud. On iOS nothing uploaded on a normal sign-in at all. Sign-in now downloads your account then pushes up everything local — including seasons, career stats and logos, which an account switch used to delete outright
1179The camera permission was asked at the wrong moment, then not at all — it fired on the Home screen before you'd picked a sport, and on the route a new user actually takes it was never requested, so you could reach the scoring screen with no camera and no prompt. Asked once now, after team and game setup
both"Enable Sharing" did nothing when signed out — no dialog, straight back to Game Setup, so it looked like it had worked. It now asks you to create an account and resumes sharing afterwards. Same hole in the YouTube upload path
bothTwo Pro features we were advertising don't exist — "Unlimited remote sessions" (remote scoring has always been free for both phones, no cap) and "Custom team names & colors" (never gated). Removed. Android was also missing six real Pro features iOS listed; both now show the same twelve
bothShare limit could be side-stepped — create a team under a sport with room, share it, then switch its sport. Sport is now fixed once a team is shared or has played
1179Coach marks work and are verified — the in-game ones were attaching invisibly, and the GO LIVE and Remote tips drew off the top of the screen. Whole new-user flow now driven automatically end to end: onboarding, both welcome tips, create-a-team, setup, permissions, rotate, all five in-game tips, scoring, recording
bothOnboarding — empty boxes now have icons (emoji in layouts silently render as nothing), page four shows Wi-Fi/Bluetooth/Internet, the Pro page said "7-day trial" in one line and "14 days" in the next, and wrapped text was cutting the bottom off letters like p and g

Shipped 17 Aug — evening

1233Transparent logos keep their shape on video — the board cropped every logo square and masked it to a circle, which cut the sides off a wide crest and undid the point of removing the background. Photos still get the round badge
1232Family link requests reach the notification bell with Approve / Deny. The request had been carrying the team and the player but not who was asking, so nothing could act on it
1231Followed-team players cleared from My Players — the local player list could only ever shrink when a player was explicitly deleted, so players arriving under another account had nothing that could remove them
1230Followed team cleared from My Teams — the cleanup pass was skipped for anyone whose cloud team list was empty, which is exactly a follower. It could not tell “you own none” from “the lookup failed”
1229A team you only follow can no longer land in My Teams — the step that rescues work made while signed out kept everything marked “not yet uploaded”, and an ordinary edit sets that mark. Also: setting a logo now uploads it immediately instead of waiting for Save
1228Assisted logo editor — crop box, pinch zoom, zoom out past the edges into transparency, one-tap background removal, eraser, and two live previews. Logo added to the FINAL card burned into the end of a recording
1228Teams you follow show up again — if you follow teams but own none, the Teams tab showed “No teams or players yet” and hid every one of them, plus the box for finding another

Known open

iOS coach marks point at the wrong things — the first has no highlight at all, the second frames Recent Games instead of the start-a-game card. Right on a used install, wrong on a fresh one, same phone — the layout is still settling when the highlight is measured
iOS can get stuck sideways — recording pins the orientation and doesn't always release it; while stuck, the in-game tips can't appear either
Husk game records; cross-device delete and tombstones don't work
Deletion-path audit across the 10 protected collections, plus a sanctioned safe-delete writer
4 orphan hubIndex entries and ~6 hubs the sweep can't reach — small reconciliation
hubPublishGated can't be removed yet — the repair machinery is still live

Settled — not up for debate again

Tiers: free shares 1 team, Pro shares 3 per sport, Club unlimited in one program
Saving teams is unlimited on every tier — the limit is on sharing, not saving
Downgrades take nothing away — limits apply only to the next new share. Don't rebuild the picker
Ship order: improve both platforms on test channels → production when solid → then the sweep
Club tier waits for a real prospect — specs are written and ready
Co-managed teams don't count against a manager — deferred into the Club design

Kept current at the end of each session, from STATUS.md in the Android repo. If this page and that file ever disagree, the file is right — tell me and I'll fix the page.
